## Canadian Energy Regulator Act {#canadian-energy-regulator-act .Title-of-Act}

[S.C.]{.abbr title="Statutes of Canada"} 2019, c. 28, s. 10
:::

Assented to 2019-06-21

An Act to establish the Canadian Energy Regulator

::: ReaderNote
\[Enacted by section 10 of chapter 28 of the Statutes of Canada, 2019,
in force August 28, 2019, see SI/2019-86.\]
:::
:::::

:::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
###### Preamble {#preamble .MarginalNote}

Whereas the Government of Canada is establishing an independent energy
regulatory body that is responsible for ensuring that pipeline, power
line and offshore renewable energy projects within Parliament's
jurisdiction are constructed, operated and abandoned in a safe and
secure manner that protects people, property and the environment;

Whereas the Government of Canada is of the opinion that the body should
be reflective and respectful of the diversity of Canada, including with
respect to the Indigenous peoples of Canada, and of its regional
diversity and bilingual nature;

Whereas the Government of Canada is committed to enhancing Canada's
global competitiveness by building a system that enables decisions to be
made in a predictable and timely manner, providing certainty to
investors and stakeholders, driving innovation and enabling the carrying
out of sound projects that create jobs for Canadians;

Whereas the Government of Canada is committed to achieving
reconciliation with First Nations, the Métis and the Inuit through
renewed nation-to-nation, government-to-government and Inuit-Crown
relationships based on recognition of rights, respect, co-operation and
partnership;

Whereas the Government of Canada is committed to implementing the United
Nations Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous Peoples;

Whereas the Government of Canada is committed to using transparent
processes that are built on early engagement and inclusive participation
and under which the best available scientific information and data as
well as Indigenous knowledge are taken into account in decision-making;

And whereas the Government of Canada is committed to assessing how
groups of women, men and gender-diverse people may experience policies,
programs and projects and to taking actions that contribute to an
inclusive and democratic society and allow all Canadians to participate
fully in all spheres of their lives;
:::

::: Enacts
Now, therefore, Her Majesty, by and with the advice and consent of the
Senate and House of Commons of Canada, enacts as follows:
:::

## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1161586 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Act may be cited as
the [Canadian Energy Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1).

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1161589 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in this Act.

[[abandoned facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[abandoned facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an abandoned
    pipeline or an abandoned offshore facility. ([installation
    abandonnée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[abandoned offshore facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[abandoned offshore facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any
    facility, equipment, system or offshore power line that has been
    abandoned in accordance with an authorization issued under Part
    5. ([installation extracôtière abandonnée]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[abandoned pipeline]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[abandoned pipeline]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a pipeline the
    operation of which has been abandoned in accordance with an order
    under subsection 241(1) and that remains in place. ([pipeline
    abandonné]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Chief Executive Officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Chief Executive Officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Chief
    Executive Officer appointed under subsection
    21(1). ([président-directeur général]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person having authority under a Special Act to
      construct or operate a pipeline; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a body corporate incorporated or continued under
      the [Canada Business Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-44) or an Act
      of the legislature of a province and not discontinued under the
      Act in question. ([compagnie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[designated officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[designated officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an employee of the
    Regulator designated under section 24. ([responsable
    désigné]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[export]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[export]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} electricity, to send from a place in Canada by a
      line of wire or other conductor electricity produced in Canada;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} oil,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to export within the meaning of any provision
        of the [Energy Administration Act](/eng/acts/E-6) that defines
        [[export]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} for the purposes of any charge
        imposed under that Act in relation to fuel for use by an
        aircraft or a vessel,

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to send or take, by any means, from a place in
        Canada, or

      - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to send or take, by any means, to a place
        outside Canada from any area of land, not within a province,
        that belongs to Her Majesty in right of Canada or in respect of
        which Her Majesty in right of Canada has the right to dispose of
        or exploit the natural resources and that is situated in
        submarine areas in the internal waters of Canada, the
        territorial sea of Canada or the continental shelf of Canada; or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} gas, to effect any one of the operations referred
      to in subparagraph (b)(ii) or
      (iii). ([exportation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any hydrocarbon or mixture of hydrocarbons that,
      at a temperature of 15°C and a pressure of 101.325 kPa, is in a
      gaseous state; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any substance designated as a gas product by
      regulations made under section 390. ([gaz]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[ground disturbance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[ground disturbance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a ground
    disturbance other than one that

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is caused by any activity that is specified in
      the orders or regulations made in respect of pipelines under
      section 335 or made in respect of international or interprovincial
      power lines under section 275;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is, in relation to a pipeline, caused by
      cultivation to a depth of less than 45 cm below the surface of the
      ground; or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is, in relation to a pipeline, caused by any
      other activity to a depth of less than 30 cm and that does not
      result in a reduction of the earth cover over the pipeline to a
      depth that is less than the cover provided when the pipeline was
      constructed. ([remuement du sol]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[hydrocarbon]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hydrocarbon]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a hydrocarbon other than
    coal. ([hydrocarbure]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[import]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[import]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to gas or oil, to
    bring into Canada through pipelines, by railway tank car, by tank
    truck or by tanker. ([importation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Indigenous governing body]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Indigenous governing body]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a council,
    government or other entity that is authorized to act on behalf of an
    Indigenous group, community or people that holds rights recognized
    and affirmed by section 35 of the [Constitution Act,
    1982](/eng/Const/). ([corps dirigeant autochtone]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[Indigenous knowledge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Indigenous knowledge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Indigenous
    knowledge of the Indigenous peoples of Canada. ([connaissances
    autochtones]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Indigenous organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Indigenous organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an Indigenous
    governing body or any other entity that represents the interests of
    an Indigenous group and its members. ([organisation
    autochtone]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Indigenous peoples of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Indigenous peoples of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning
    assigned by the definition [[aboriginal peoples of
    Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 35(2) of the
    [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/). ([peuples autochtones du
    Canada]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[inspection officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[inspection officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person designated
    under subsection 102(1). ([inspecteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[international power line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[international power line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means facilities
    that are constructed or operated for the purpose of transmitting
    electricity from a place in Canada to a place outside Canada or from
    a place outside Canada to a place in Canada. It does not however
    include an offshore power line. ([ligne internationale de transport
    d'électricité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"} ou [ligne
    internationale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[interprovincial power line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[interprovincial power line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means facilities
    that are constructed or operated for the purpose of transmitting
    electricity from a place in a province to a place in another
    province. ([ligne interprovinciale de transport
    d'électricité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"} ou [ligne
    interprovinciale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[land registrar]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[land registrar]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an officer with whom
    titles, rights or interests relating to real property or immovables
    are registered or recorded. ([directeur de
    l'enregistrement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[lands]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[lands]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes real property and any
    interest or right in real property or land and, in Quebec, any
    immovable, any right in an immovable and the right of a lessee in
    respect of any immovable. Those interests or rights may be in, to,
    on, under, over or in respect of the
    lands. ([terrains]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the member of the Queen's
    Privy Council for Canada designated under section
    8. ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[navigable water]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[navigable water]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    section 2 of the [Canadian Navigable Waters
    Act](/eng/acts/N-22). ([eaux navigables]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[offshore area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[offshore area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the part of the internal waters of Canada or of
      the territorial sea of Canada that is not situated in

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a province other than the Northwest
        Territories, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the [[onshore]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as
        defined in section 2 of the [Northwest Territories
        Act](/eng/acts/N-27.05); and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the continental shelf of Canada and the waters
      superjacent to the seabed of that shelf. ([zone
      extracôtière]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[offshore power line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[offshore power line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means facilities
    constructed or operated for the purpose of transmitting electricity
    from an offshore renewable energy project to a province or a place
    outside Canada. ([ligne extracôtière]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[offshore renewable energy project]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[offshore renewable energy project]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any
    of the following that are carried on in the offshore area:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any research or assessment conducted in relation
      to the exploitation or potential exploitation of a renewable
      resource to produce energy;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any exploitation of a renewable resource to
      produce energy;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any storage of energy produced from a renewable
      resource; or

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any transmission of such energy, other than the
      transmission of electricity to a province or a place outside
      Canada. ([projet d'énergie renouvelable
      extracôtière]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any hydrocarbon or mixture of hydrocarbons other
      than gas; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any substance designated as an oil product by
      regulations made under section 390. ([pétrole]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[penalty]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[penalty]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an administrative monetary
    penalty imposed under this Act for a
    violation. ([pénalité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[pipeline]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[pipeline]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a line --- including all
    branches, extensions, tanks, reservoirs, storage or loading
    facilities, pumps, racks, compressors, interstation communication
    systems, real or personal property, or immovable or movable, and any
    connected works --- that connects at least two provinces or extends
    beyond the limits of a province, Sable Island or an area referred to
    in paragraph (c) of the definition [[designated
    area]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 368 and that is used or is
    to be used for the transmission of oil, gas or any other commodity.
    It does not however include a sewer or water pipeline that is used
    or is to be used solely for municipal
    purposes. ([pipeline]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[power line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[power line]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an international power
    line, an interprovincial power line or an offshore power
    line. ([ligne de transport d'électricité]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[regulated facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[regulated facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a pipeline, an
    international power line, an interprovincial power line designated
    by an order made under section 261, an offshore power line, or any
    facility, equipment or system that is related to an offshore
    renewable energy project. ([installation
    réglementée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Regulator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Regulator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the corporation established
    under subsection 10(1). ([Régie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Special Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Special Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an Act of Parliament that authorizes a person
      named in the Act to construct or operate a pipeline or that is
      enacted with special reference to a pipeline that, by such an Act,
      a person is authorized to construct or operate; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} letters patent issued under section 5.1 or 5.4 of
      the [Canada Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-1.8), chapter C-32 of
      the Revised Statutes of Canada, 1970, except for the purpose of
      paragraph 342(b) of this Act. ([loi spéciale]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[toll]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[toll]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes any rate, charge or allowance
    charged or made

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the shipment, transportation, transmission,
      care, handling or delivery of hydrocarbons or of another commodity
      that is transmitted through a pipeline, or for storage or
      demurrage, or the like;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the provision of a pipeline when the pipeline
      is available and ready to provide for the transmission of oil or
      gas; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the purchase and sale of gas that
      is the property of a company and that is transmitted by the
      company through its pipeline, excluding the cost to the company of
      the gas at the point where it enters the
      pipeline. ([droit]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2024, c. 30,
s. 34\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-3-20190828.html "Link to previous version of section 3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application --- Special Act lands

**[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** A provision of this Act or
a regulation made under it that applies to lands also applies to lands
referred to in a Special Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of liquidators, trustees, etc.

- **[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the
  purposes of this Act, each of the following is considered to be a
  company:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a liquidator, receiver or manager of the property
    of a company, appointed by a court of competent jurisdiction to
    carry on the business of the company;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a trustee --- or the holder of a power of attorney
    within the meaning of the Civil Code of Québec --- for the holders
    of bonds, debentures, debenture stock or other evidence of
    indebtedness of the company, secured under a trust deed, an act
    constituting a hypothec within the meaning of the Civil Code of
    Québec or other instrument or act, on or against the property of the
    company, if the trustee or holder is authorized by the instrument or
    act to carry on the business of the company; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person, other than a company,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} operating a pipeline constructed before October
      1, 1953, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} constructing, operating or abandoning a pipeline
      exempted from subsection 179(1) by an order made by the Commission
      under subsection 214(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrator in Quebec

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In Quebec, the administrator of the property of the
  company appointed by a court of competent jurisdiction to carry on the
  business of the company is also considered to be the company.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Successor or assign --- abandoned
  pipeline

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act, a successor or assign
  of a company is considered to be a company for any matter relating to
  an abandoned pipeline.

## [Purpose]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1161669 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of Act

**[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** The purpose of this Act is
to regulate certain energy matters within Parliament's jurisdiction and,
in particular,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to ensure that pipelines and power lines as well as
  facilities, equipment or systems related to offshore renewable energy
  projects, are constructed, operated and abandoned in a manner that is
  safe, secure and efficient and that protects people, property and the
  environment;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to ensure that the exploration for and exploitation
  of [[oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} and [[gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as
  defined in section 2 of the [Canada Oil and Gas Operations
  Act](/eng/acts/O-7), is carried out in a manner that is safe and
  secure and that protects people, property and the environment;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} to regulate trade in energy products; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} to ensure that regulatory hearings and
  decision-making processes related to those energy matters are fair,
  inclusive, transparent and efficient.

## [General]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1161676 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Binding on Her Majesty

**[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** This Act is binding on Her
Majesty in right of Canada or a province.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order designating Minister

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, by order, designate any member of the Queen's Privy Council for
Canada to be the Minister for the purposes this Act.

## [PART 1]{.HLabel1}[Canadian Energy Regulator]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1161681 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[document of
authorization]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** In this Part, [[document of
authorization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a certificate issued under
Part 3 or 4, a permit issued under Part 4 or 7, an authorization issued
under Part 5, a licence issued under Part 7 or an order made under
section 214.

### [Establishment and Mandate]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1161684 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian Energy Regulator

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  corporation is established to be called the Canadian Energy Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agent of Her Majesty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator is for all purposes an agent of Her
  Majesty in right of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Head office and other offices

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The head office of the Regulator is to be in Calgary,
  Alberta. The Regulator's Chief Executive Officer may open or close
  other offices after consulting the board of directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the opening or closing of an
  office does not change the conditions of appointment of a director or
  commissioner.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandate

**[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator's mandate
includes

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} making transparent decisions, orders and
  recommendations with respect to pipelines, power lines, offshore
  renewable energy projects and abandoned pipelines;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} overseeing the construction, operation and
  abandonment of pipelines, interprovincial power lines and
  international power lines and overseeing work and activities
  authorized under Part 5 as well as abandoned facilities;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} making orders with respect to traffic, tolls and
  tariffs and overseeing matters relating to traffic, tolls and tariffs;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} making decisions and orders and giving directions
  under Part 8 with respect to oil and gas interests, production and
  conservation;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} advising and reporting on energy matters;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} providing alternative dispute resolution processes;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} exercising powers and performing duties and functions
  that are conferred on the Regulator under any other Act of Parliament;
  and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} exercising its powers and performing its duties and
  functions in a manner that respects the Government of Canada's
  commitments with respect to the rights of the Indigenous peoples of
  Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction --- Inuvialuit Settlement
Region

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Until
  March 31, 2034, the Regulator is to be the regulator --- under any law
  of the Legislature of the Northwest Territories that is made under
  paragraph 19(1)(a), (b) or (c) of the [Northwest Territories
  Act](/eng/acts/N-27.05) --- in respect of that portion of the
  [[Inuvialuit Settlement Region]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in
  section 2 of the [Canada Oil and Gas Operations Act](/eng/acts/O-7),
  that is situated in the [[onshore]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined
  in section 2 of the [Northwest Territories Act](/eng/acts/N-27.05).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Successive periods and termination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After March 31, 2034, the Government of Canada and
  the Government of the Northwest Territories may agree that the
  Regulator is to continue to be the regulator for one or more periods
  of 20 years each; they may also, before the expiry of each successive
  period, agree to its earlier termination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Governor in Council directions

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, by order, give to the Regulator directions of
  general application on broad policy matters with respect to the
  Regulator's mandate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions binding

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) is binding on the
  Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication and tabling

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A copy of each order made under subsection (1) must
  be published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and
  tabled in each House of Parliament.

### [Board of Directors]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1161716 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment and composition

- **[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator is to have a board of directors consisting of at least five
  but not more than nine directors, including a Chairperson and a
  Vice-Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Indigenous representation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At least one of the directors must be an Indigenous
  person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

- **[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chairperson, Vice-Chairperson and the other directors are to be
  appointed by the Governor in Council to hold office on a part-time
  basis and during pleasure for a term not exceeding five years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reappointment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A director may be reappointed in the same or another
  capacity for one or more terms of up to five years each.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligibility --- directors

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person is not eligible to be a director if they are
  the Chief Executive Officer, a commissioner or an employee of the
  Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligibility --- Chairperson and
  Vice-Chairperson

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person is not eligible to be Chairperson or
  Vice-Chairperson unless they are a Canadian citizen or [[permanent
  resident]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1) of the
  [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A director is to be paid the remuneration that is
  fixed by the Governor in Council and is entitled to be paid reasonable
  travel and other expenses while performing their duties and functions
  under this Act while absent from their ordinary place of residence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Conflict of Interest
Act](/eng/acts/C-36.65)

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of the
[Conflict of Interest Act](/eng/acts/C-36.65), the circumstances in
which a director is in a conflict of interest while exercising the
powers or performing the duties and functions of a director include

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} engaging in, as owner, shareholder, director,
  officer, partner or in any other way, the business of producing,
  selling, buying, transmitting, exporting, importing or otherwise
  dealing in hydrocarbons, electricity or offshore energy;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} holding any bond, debenture or other security of a
  corporation engaged in any such business; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} holding other employment that is inconsistent with
  their powers, duties and functions or with any provision of this Act
  or a regulation made under it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Role of the board of directors

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  board of directors is responsible for the governance of the Regulator
  and its governance functions include providing strategic direction and
  advice to the Regulator. The board of directors must not however give
  directions or provide advice with respect to any particular decision,
  order or recommendation that is made by the Commission or a
  commissioner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-laws

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The board of directors may make by-laws respecting
  the conduct of its meetings and the general conduct of its activities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A majority of the directors holding office, including
  the Chairperson, constitutes a quorum of the board of directors.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report

- **[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  board of directors must, within 120 days after the end of each fiscal
  year, submit to the Minister a report on the Regulator's activities
  under this Act for that fiscal year, and the Minister must cause the
  report to be laid before each House of Parliament on any of the first
  15 days on which that House is sitting after the day on which the
  report is received.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other reports

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The board of directors may submit a report to the
  Minister on the Regulator's activities if the board considers it
  appropriate to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[fiscal
  year]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), [[fiscal
  year]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the period beginning on April 1 in
  one year and ending on March 31 in the next year.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Role of Chairperson

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chairperson presides over meetings of the board of directors and may
  perform any duties or functions that are assigned to him or her by the
  board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acting Chairperson

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Chairperson is absent or is unable to act or
  if there is a vacancy in that office, the Vice-Chairperson acts as
  Chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Governor in Council approval

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Vice-Chairperson is not authorized to act as
  Chairperson for a period of more than 90 days without the approval of
  the Governor in Council.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacancy

**[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** A vacancy in the board of
directors does not impair the right of the remaining directors to act.

### [Chief Executive Officer]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1161761 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

- **[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief Executive Officer of the Regulator is to be appointed by the
  Governor in Council on the recommendation of the Minister after the
  Minister has consulted the directors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Executive Officer is to hold office on a
  full-time basis during pleasure for a term of up to six years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reappointment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Executive Officer may be reappointed for
  one or more terms of up to six years each. However, the Chief
  Executive Officer is to serve no more than 10 years in office in
  total.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligibility

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person is not eligible to be the Chief Executive
  Officer unless they are a Canadian citizen or [[permanent
  resident]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1) of the
  [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligibility

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A director is not eligible to be the Chief Executive
  Officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Executive Officer is to be paid the
  remuneration that is fixed by the Governor in Council and is entitled
  to be paid reasonable travel and other expenses while performing his
  or her duties and functions under this Act while absent from his or
  her ordinary place of work.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Conflict of Interest
Act](/eng/acts/C-36.65)

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of the
[Conflict of Interest Act](/eng/acts/C-36.65), the circumstances in
which the Chief Executive Officer is in a conflict of interest while
exercising the powers or performing the duties and functions of the
Chief Executive Officer include

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} engaging in, as owner, shareholder, director,
  officer, partner or in any other way, the business of producing,
  selling, buying, transmitting, exporting, importing or otherwise
  dealing in hydrocarbons, electricity or offshore energy;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} holding any bond, debenture or other security of a
  corporation engaged in any such business;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} holding other employment that is inconsistent with
  the Chief Executive Officer's powers, duties and functions or with any
  provision of this Act or a regulation made under it; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} holding office as a commissioner or being employed by
  the Regulator.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Role of Chief Executive Officer

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief Executive Officer is responsible for the management of the
  Regulator's day-to-day business and affairs, including the supervision
  of its employees and their work. The Chief Executive Officer must not
  however give directions with respect to any particular decision, order
  or recommendation that is made by the Commission or a commissioner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rank of deputy head

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Executive Officer has the rank and the
  powers of a deputy head of a department.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acting Chief Executive Officer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Chief Executive Officer is absent or unable to
  act or if there is a vacancy in that office, an executive of the
  Regulator designated by the Minister acts as Chief Executive Officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Governor in Council approval

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The executive referred to in subsection (3) is not
  authorized to act as Chief Executive Officer for a period of more than
  90 days without the approval of the Governor in Council.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designated officers

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** The Chief Executive
Officer may designate employees of the Regulator as designated officers.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
the Chief Executive Officer is responsible for the provision of the
support services and the facilities that are needed by the Commission to
exercise its powers and perform its duties and functions in accordance
with the rules that apply to its work.

### [Commission]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1161794 .Subheading}

#### [Composition and Appointments]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1161795 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commission

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator is to have a Commission that consists of up to seven
  full-time commissioners. It may also include a complement of part-time
  commissioners.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Indigenous representation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At least one of the full-time commissioners must be
  an Indigenous person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum

**[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** Subject to subsection
42(2), sections 45 to 47 and subsection 48(2), three commissioners
constitutes a quorum of the Commission.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment

- **[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  commissioners are to be appointed by the Governor in Council to hold
  office during good behaviour for a term not exceeding six years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reappointment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A commissioner may be reappointed for one or more
  terms of up to six years each. However, a commissioner is to serve no
  more than 10 years in office in total.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may remove a commissioner for
  cause.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligibility

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person is not eligible to be a commissioner unless
  they are a Canadian citizen or [[permanent
  resident]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1) of the
  [Immigration and Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligibility

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A director is not eligible to be a commissioner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses --- full-time
  commissioner

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A full-time commissioner is to be paid the
  remuneration that is fixed by the Governor in Council and is entitled
  to be paid reasonable travel and other expenses while performing their
  duties and functions under this Act while absent from their ordinary
  place of work.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses --- part-time
  commissioner

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A part-time commissioner is to be paid the
  remuneration that is fixed by the Governor in Council and is entitled
  to be paid reasonable travel and other expenses while performing their
  duties and functions under this Act while absent from their ordinary
  place of residence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Conflict of Interest
Act](/eng/acts/C-36.65)

**[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of the
[Conflict of Interest Act](/eng/acts/C-36.65), the circumstances in
which a commissioner is in a conflict of interest while exercising the
powers or performing the duties and functions of a commissioner include

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} engaging in, as owner, shareholder, director,
  officer, partner or in any other way, the business of producing,
  selling, buying, transmitting, exporting, importing or otherwise
  dealing in hydrocarbons, electricity or offshore energy;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} holding any bond, debenture or other security of a
  corporation engaged in any such business;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} holding other employment that is inconsistent with
  their powers, duties and functions or with any provision of this Act
  or a regulation made under it; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} holding office as the Chief Executive Officer or
  being employed by the Regulator.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacancy

**[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** A vacancy in the
Commission does not impair the right of the remaining commissioners to
act.

#### [Powers, Duties and Functions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1161826 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court of record

- **[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commission is a court of record and the Regulator's seal must be
  judicially noticed as the Commission's official seal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commission has all the powers, rights and
  privileges vested in a superior court of record with respect to any
  matters within its jurisdiction, including the attendance, swearing
  and examination of witnesses, the production and inspection of
  documents, the enforcement of its orders and the entry on and
  inspection of property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expeditious applications and
  proceedings

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} All applications and proceedings before the
  Commission must be dealt with as expeditiously as the circumstances
  and procedural fairness and natural justice permit, but, in any case,
  within any time limit provided for under this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commission has full and exclusive jurisdiction to inquire into, hear
  and determine any matter if the Commission considers that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person has failed to do anything that is required
    to be done under this Act, under a condition of a document of
    authorization, or under an order made or direction given under this
    Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person has done or is doing anything that is
    contrary to or in contravention of this Act, a condition of a
    document of authorization or an order made or direction given under
    this Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances may require the Commission, in
    the public interest, to make any order or give any direction, leave,
    sanction or approval that it is authorized to make or give, or that
    relates to anything that is prohibited, sanctioned or required to be
    done under this Act, a condition of a document of authorization, or
    an order made or direction given under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commission may inquire into any accident
  involving a pipeline, abandoned pipeline, international power line,
  offshore renewable energy project, offshore power line or other
  facility the construction or operation of which is regulated by the
  Regulator and may, at the conclusion of the inquiry, make

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} findings as to the cause of the accident or factors
    contributing to it;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} recommendations relating to the prevention of
    future similar accidents; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any decision or order that the Commission is
    authorized to make.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Matters of law and fact

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act, the Commission has full
  jurisdiction to hear and determine all matters, whether of law or of
  fact.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to act on own initiative

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** The Commission may on its
own initiative inquire into, hear and determine any matter that under
this Act it may inquire into, hear and determine.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders and prohibitions

**[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** The Commission may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} order any person to do, immediately or within or at
  any specified time and in any specified manner, anything that the
  person is or may be required to do under this Act, under a condition
  of a document of authorization, or under any order made or direction
  given by the Commission or a designated officer under this Act; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} prohibit the doing or continuing of anything that is
  contrary to this Act, to a condition of the document of authorization
  or to the order or direction.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules

**[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** The Commission may make
rules generally for the carrying out of its work and the management of
its internal affairs, including rules respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the powers, duties and functions of the
  commissioners;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} its sittings;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} its decisions, orders and recommendations; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} its procedures and practices.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commission must, within 120 days after the end of each fiscal year,
  submit to the Minister a report on the Commission's activities under
  this Act for that fiscal year, and the Minister must cause the report
  to be laid before each House of Parliament on any of the first 15 days
  on which that House is sitting after the day on which the report is
  received.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other reports

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commission may submit a report to the Minister on
  the Commission's activities if the Commission considers it appropriate
  to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[fiscal
  year]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), [[fiscal
  year]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the period beginning on April 1 in
  one year and ending on March 31 in the next year.

#### [Lead Commissioner and Deputy Lead Commissioner]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1161866 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

**[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
must designate one of the full-time commissioners to hold office as Lead
Commissioner and one of the full-time commissioners to hold office as
Deputy Lead Commissioner.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Role of Lead Commissioner

**[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** The Lead Commissioner is
responsible for the business and affairs of the Commission and, in
particular, is responsible for apportioning the Commission's work among
the commissioners and for establishing panels --- of at least three
commissioners --- to exercise the powers of the Commission and perform
its duties and functions in relation to a matter before it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acting Lead Commissioner

- **[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Deputy Lead Commissioner acts as Lead Commissioner if the Lead
  Commissioner is absent or unable to act or if there is a vacancy in
  that office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Governor in Council approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Deputy Lead Commissioner is not authorized to act
  as Lead Commissioner for a period of more than 90 days without the
  approval of the Governor in Council.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acting Lead and Deputy Lead Commissioners

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  both the Lead Commissioner and Deputy Lead Commissioner are absent or
  unable to act or if there are vacancies in both of those offices, a
  commissioner designated by the Minister acts as Lead Commissioner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Governor in Council approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The designated commissioner is not authorized to act
  as Lead Commissioner for a period of more than 90 days without the
  approval of the Governor in Council.

#### [Instructions and Measures]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1161881 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Instructions regarding timeliness

**[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** To ensure that an
application before the Commission is dealt with in a timely manner, the
Lead Commissioner may give instructions to the commissioners authorized
to deal with the application respecting the manner in which they are to
do so.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measures to meet time limit

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  Lead Commissioner is satisfied that a time limit imposed under any of
  sections 183, 214 and 262 is not likely to be met in respect of an
  application, the Lead Commissioner may take any measure that he or she
  considers appropriate to ensure that the time limit is met, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} removing any or all commissioners from a panel
    authorized to deal with the application;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorizing one or more commissioners to deal with
    the application; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} increasing or decreasing the number of
    commissioners dealing with the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the power referred to in
  subsection (1) includes the power to designate a single member,
  including the Lead Commissioner, as the sole commissioner who is
  authorized to deal with the application.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inconsistencies

**[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** In the event of any
inconsistency between a instruction given under section 41 or a measure
taken under subsection 42(1) and any rule made under section 35, the
instruction or measure prevails to the extent of the inconsistency.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

**[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
sections 41 and 42 do not apply to an application with respect to a
[[designated project]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 2 of
the [Impact Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75), that is subject to an
impact assessment under that Act.

#### [Authorizations, Panels and Hearings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1161896 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization --- powers, duties and
functions

- **[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Lead Commissioner may authorize one or more commissioners, either
  jointly or severally, to exercise any of the powers of the Commission
  or to perform any of its duties and functions under this Act, except
  those under sections 95, 183, 184, 186, 195 to 197, subsection 211(3),
  sections 212 to 214, 231 to 234, 238 to 240, 248, 257, 262, 274, 285
  to 287, 289 and under Parts 7 and 8.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A power exercised or a duty or function performed
  under the authorization is considered to have been exercised or
  performed by the Commission.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization --- report

- **[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Lead Commissioner may authorize one or more of the commissioners to
  report to the Commission on any matter related to the work of the
  Commission or any application or proceeding before it. For the purpose
  of making a report related to an application or proceeding before the
  Commission, the authorized commissioners have all the powers of the
  Commission with respect to taking evidence or acquiring information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not a quorum

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 27 of this Act and paragraph 22(2)(a)
  of the [Interpretation Act](/eng/acts/I-21), three or more
  commissioners authorized to report in accordance with subsection (1)
  do not constitute a quorum of the Commission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A report to the Commission under subsection (1) may
  be adopted as a decision or order of the Commission or be dealt with
  as the Commission considers appropriate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacement of commissioner during
hearing

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  commissioner who is conducting a hearing in accordance with an
  authorization under section 45 or 46 is unable to act or resigns
  during the hearing, the Lead Commissioner may authorize another
  commissioner to continue the hearing and to make the decision or
  report.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacement of commissioner before
  decision or report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a commissioner who is conducting a hearing in
  accordance with an authorization under section 45 or 46 is unable to
  act or resigns after the conclusion of the hearing but before the
  decision or report is made, the Lead Commissioner may authorize
  another commissioner to examine all the evidence presented at the
  hearing and to make the decision or report on the basis of that
  evidence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacement of panel member during
hearing

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  panel is conducting a hearing and a commissioner on the panel is
  unable to act or resigns during the hearing, the Lead Commissioner may
  authorize another commissioner to replace that commissioner for the
  rest of the hearing and to participate in the decision or, as
  applicable, finalize the report required under subsection 183(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacement of panel member before
  decision or report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a panel is conducting a hearing and a commissioner
  on the panel is unable to act or resigns after the conclusion of the
  hearing but before the decision is made or, as applicable, the report
  required under subsection 183(1) is finalized, the remaining
  commissioners may --- if unanimous --- make the decision or finalize
  the report as if the commissioner were present and participating.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lead Commissioner's powers

**[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in section 47 or
subsection 48(1) precludes the Lead Commissioner from taking a measure
under subsection 42(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change in composition of panel

**[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** If the composition of a
panel changes in accordance with subsection 42(1) or 48(1),

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} evidence and representations received by the
  Commission in relation to the matter being dealt with by the panel
  before the change are considered to have been received after the
  change; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Commission is bound by every decision made by the
  Commission in relation to the matter being dealt with by the panel
  before the change unless the Commission elects to review, vary or
  rescind it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization to continue

**[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** If a commissioner resigns
or their term expires, the Lead Commissioner may, on any conditions he
or she specifies, authorize the former commissioner to continue to
exercise powers and perform duties and functions as a commissioner in a
matter to which they were assigned immediately before resigning or the
expiry of their term until a final decision or recommendation in the
matter is made, and the former commissioner is considered to be a
commissioner for the purposes of this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public hearings

- **[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  hearing before the Commission with respect to the issuance, suspension
  or revocation of a certificate under Part 3 or 4 must be public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a hearing with
  respect to the suspension or revocation of either of the following
  certificates if the holder applies for or consents to the suspension
  or revocation:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a certificate issued in respect of an international
    or interprovincial power line, whether or not the power line has
    been brought into commercial operation under the certificate; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a certificate issued in respect of a pipeline that
    has not been brought into commercial operation under the
    certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other matters

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Commission may hold a public hearing in respect
  of any other matter if the Commission considers it appropriate to do
  so and shall make public its reasons for holding the hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, subsection (1) does not affect
  the operation of subsection 241(3).

#### [Recommendations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1161938 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

- **[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commission must issue written reasons for each recommendation it makes
  to the Governor in Council or the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public availability

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator must make the recommendations, and the
  reasons for them, publicly available.

### [Exercise of Commission's Powers and Performance of Its Duties and Functions by Designated Officers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1161944 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations that specify

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} powers, duties and functions of the Commission that
  are technical or administrative in nature and may be exercised or
  performed by designated officers;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any circumstances in which those powers are to be
  exercised and those duties and functions are to be performed only by
  designated officers; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the procedures and practices that apply to the
  exercise of those powers and the performance of those duties and
  functions by designated officers.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Apportionment of work

**[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** The Chief Executive
Officer is responsible for apportioning among the designated officers
any work related to a power, duty or function that is specified in a
regulation made under section 54.

### [Rights and Interests of the Indigenous Peoples of Canada]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1161952 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to consider --- Commission

- **[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  making a decision, an order or a recommendation under this Act, the
  Commission must consider any adverse effects that the decision, order
  or recommendation may have on the rights of the Indigenous peoples of
  Canada recognized and affirmed by section 35 of the [Constitution Act,
  1982](/eng/Const/).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to consider --- designated
  officers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When making a decision or an order under this Act, a
  designated officer must consider any adverse effects that the decision
  or order may have on the rights of the Indigenous peoples of Canada
  recognized and affirmed by section 35 of the [Constitution Act,
  1982](/eng/Const/).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advisory committee

- **[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator must establish an advisory committee for the purpose of
  enhancing the involvement, under Part 2, of the Indigenous peoples of
  Canada and Indigenous organizations in respect of pipelines, power
  lines and offshore renewable energy projects as well as abandoned
  pipelines.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Membership

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The membership of the advisory committee must include
  at least

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one person recommended by an Indigenous
    organization that represents the interests of First Nations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one person recommended by an Indigenous
    organization that represents the interests of the Inuit; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} one person recommended by an Indigenous
    organization that represents the interests of the Métis.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality --- Indigenous knowledge

- **[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  Indigenous knowledge that is provided in confidence to the Regulator
  under this Act or any other Act of Parliament that confers powers,
  duties or functions on the Regulator is confidential and must not
  knowingly be, or be permitted to be, disclosed without written
  consent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the Indigenous knowledge
  referred to in that subsection may be disclosed if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is publicly available;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure is necessary for the purposes of
    procedural fairness and natural justice or for use in legal
    proceedings; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure is authorized in the circumstances
    set out in the regulations made under section 59.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Before disclosing Indigenous knowledge under
  paragraph 2(b) for the purposes of procedural fairness and natural
  justice, the Regulator must consult the person or entity who provided
  the Indigenous knowledge and the person or entity to whom it is
  proposed to be disclosed about the scope of the proposed disclosure
  and potential conditions under subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further disclosure

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may, having regard to the consultation
  referred to in subsection (2.1), impose conditions with respect to the
  disclosure of Indigenous knowledge by any person or entity to whom it
  is disclosed under paragraph (2)(b) for the purposes of procedural
  fairness and natural justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to comply

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The person or entity referred to in subsection (3)
  must comply with any conditions imposed by the Regulator under that
  subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection from civil proceeding or
  prosecution

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other Act of Parliament, no civil or
  criminal proceedings lie against the Regulator or the Minister --- or
  any person acting on behalf of, or under the direction of, either of
  them --- and no proceedings lie against the Crown or the Regulator,
  for the disclosure in good faith of any Indigenous knowledge under
  this Act or any other Act of Parliament that confers powers, duties or
  functions on the Regulator or for any consequences that flow from that
  disclosure.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations prescribing the circumstances in which Indigenous
knowledge that is provided to the Regulator under this Act in confidence
may be disclosed without written consent.

### [Confidentiality of Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1161984 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality

**[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** The Commission or a
designated officer may take any measures and make any order that the
Commission or designated officer considers necessary to ensure the
confidentiality of any information likely to be disclosed in any
proceedings under this Act if the Commission or designated officer is
satisfied that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} disclosure of the information could reasonably be
  expected to result in a material loss or gain to a person directly
  affected by the proceedings, or could reasonably be expected to
  prejudice the person's competitive position;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the information is financial, commercial, scientific
  or technical information that is confidential information provided to
  the Regulator and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the information has been consistently treated as
    confidential information by a person directly affected by the
    proceedings, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the Commission or designated officer considers
    that the person's interest in confidentiality outweighs the public
    interest in disclosure of the proceedings; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} there is a real and substantial risk that disclosure
  of the information will compromise the safety and well-being of
  persons or cause harm to property or the environment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality

**[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** The Commission or a
designated officer may take any measures and make any order that the
Commission or designated officer considers necessary to ensure the
confidentiality of information that is contained in any order under this
Act or that is likely to be disclosed in any proceedings under this Act
if the Commission or designated officer is satisfied that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is a real and substantial risk that disclosure
  of the information will pose a risk to the security of pipelines,
  abandoned pipelines, power lines, offshore renewable energy projects,
  including buildings, structures or systems --- including computer or
  communication systems, or methods employed to protect them --- and the
  need to prevent disclosure of the information outweighs the public
  interest in disclosure of orders and proceedings; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is a real and substantial risk that disclosure
  of the information will compromise the safety and well-being of
  persons or cause harm to property or the environment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality

**[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** The Commission or a
designated officer may take any measures and make any orders that are
necessary to ensure compliance with section 58 or any regulations
referred in subsection 114(3).

### [Decisions and Orders]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1161998 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1161999 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commission must issue written reasons for each decision or order it
  makes. A designated officer must do the same for each decision or
  order they make.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public availability

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator must make the decisions and orders, and
  the reasons for them, publicly available.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply to a decision or order
  that is made only in relation to the internal administrative affairs
  of the Regulator.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Enforcement of orders

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  decision or order of the Commission, a designated officer or an
  inspection officer may be made an order of the Federal Court or of a
  superior court of a province and may be enforced in the same manner as
  an order of that court as if it had been an order of that court on the
  date of the decision or order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A decision or order of the Commission, a designated
  officer or an inspection officer may be made an order of a court in
  accordance with the usual practice and procedure of the court in such
  matters, if any, or by the filing with the registrar of the court of a
  copy of the decision certified by an employee of the Regulator who is
  authorized by the Chief Executive Officer to certify documents for the
  purposes of this section under the Regulator's seal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of revocation or amendment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a decision or order of the Commission, a
  designated officer or an inspection officer that has been made an
  order of a court is rescinded or varied by a subsequent decision or
  order, the order of the court is vacated and the decision or order of
  the Commission, a designated officer or an inspection officer as
  varied may be made an order of the court in accordance with subsection
  (2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

**[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** A decision or order made
by the Commission, a designated officer or an inspection officer under
this Act may include any conditions that the Commission, designated
officer or inspection officer considers appropriate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General or particular application

**[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** The Commission or a
designated officer may, when making an order, giving a direction,
imposing conditions or doing any other thing in relation to a person, do
so either generally or in any particular case or class of cases.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Coming into force and cessation of effect
--- Commission

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commission may, in issuing, varying or transferring a document of
  authorization, or when making or varying an order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} specify a day on which the document of
    authorization or order, or any part of it, comes into force or
    ceases to have effect; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} impose conditions governing when the document of
    authorization or order, or any part of it, comes into force or
    ceases to have effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Coming into force and cessation of
  effect --- designated officer or inspection officer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A designated officer or inspection officer may, when
  making or varying an order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} specify a day on which the order, or any part of
    it, comes into force or ceases to have effect; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} impose conditions governing when the order, or any
    part of it, comes into force or ceases to have effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a certificate issued
  under Part 3.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim orders

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Commission, a designated officer or inspection
  officer may, instead of making an order final in the first instance,
  make an interim order, and may reserve their decision pending further
  proceedings in connection with any matter.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relief

- **[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commission may make a decision or order that grants, in whole or in
  part, any application made to the Commission. The decision or order
  may also include --- in addition to or in lieu of the relief applied
  for --- any other relief that the Commission considers appropriate as
  if the application had been made for that other relief.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to an application made
  under Part 3 for a certificate.

#### [Reviews and Appeals]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162036 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to review, vary or rescind ---
Commission

- **[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commission may review, vary or rescind any decision or order it makes
  and, if applicable, may re-hear any application before deciding it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to vary or rescind --- designated
  officer or inspection officer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A designated officer or inspection officer may vary
  or rescind any decision or order they make and, if applicable, may
  re-hear any application before deciding it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply to a decision or order in
  relation to an operating licence or authorization to which section 382
  or 383 applies or in relation to the approval of a development plan
  under section 5.1 of the [Canada Oil and Gas Operations
  Act](/eng/acts/O-7).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decisions final

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except
  as provided for in this Act, every decision or order of the
  Commission, a designated officer or an inspection officer is final and
  conclusive.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision or order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any minute or other record of the Commission, a
  designated officer or an inspection officer or any document issued by
  one of them, in the form of a decision or order, is considered for the
  purposes of this section to be a decision or order, as the case may
  be, of the Commission, the designated officer or the inspection
  officer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal to Commission

**[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** An appeal from a decision
or order of a designated officer or an inspection officer may be brought
before the Commission and the Commission may dismiss or allow the appeal
and vary or rescind the decision or order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal to Federal Court of Appeal

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  appeal from a decision or order of the Commission on any question of
  law or of jurisdiction may be brought in the Federal Court of Appeal
  with the leave of that Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for leave to appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Leave to appeal must be applied for within 30 days
  after the date of the decision or order appealed from or within any
  additional time that a judge of the Court grants in exceptional
  circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit for appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An appeal must be brought within 60 days after the
  day on which leave to appeal is granted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Argument by Regulator

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator is entitled to be heard on an
  application for leave to appeal and at any stage of an appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In any appeal under this section, costs may not be
  awarded against any of the commissioners.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report not decision or order

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a report submitted by the
  Commission under section 183 or 184 --- or under subsection 51(1) the
  [Impact Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75) --- is not a decision or
  order of the Commission for the purposes of this section and neither
  is any part of the report.

### [Alternative Dispute Resolution]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162064 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative dispute resolution

- **[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If all
  the parties to a dispute that is directly related to a matter under
  this Act consent, the Regulator must provide or cause to be provided
  an alternative dispute resolution process for the dispute.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Results

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The results of the alternative dispute resolution
  process are not binding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of results by Commission

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Commission may take the results of the
  alternative dispute resolution process into account when making a
  decision, order or recommendation and may refer to them in the
  decision, order or recommendation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of results by designated officer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A designated officer may take the results of the
  alternative dispute resolution process into account when making a
  decision or order and may refer to them in the decision or order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public availability of results

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may make the results of the alternative
  dispute resolution process public with the consent of the parties.

### [Public Engagement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162076 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public engagement

**[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator must
establish processes that the Regulator considers appropriate to engage
meaningfully with the public --- and, in particular, the Indigenous
peoples of Canada and Indigenous organizations --- when public hearings
are held under section 52 or subsection 241(3).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Participant funding program

**[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of this
Act, the Regulator must establish a participant funding program to
facilitate the participation of the public --- and, in particular, the
Indigenous peoples of Canada and Indigenous organizations --- in public
hearings under section 52 or subsection 241(3) and any steps leading to
those hearings.

### [Collaborative Processes and Ministerial Arrangements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162081 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collaborative processes

**[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator may enter
into arrangements with any government or Indigenous organization to
establish collaborative processes.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial arrangements

- **[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  regulations are made under section 78, the Minister may, in accordance
  with those regulations, enter into arrangements with Indigenous
  governing bodies for carrying out the purposes of this Act and may
  authorize any Indigenous governing body with whom an arrangement is
  entered into to exercise the powers or perform the duties and
  functions under this Act that are specified in the arrangement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Within 30 days after the day on which the Minister
  enters into an arrangement, the Regulator must publish it on the
  Regulator's website.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may make regulations respecting the Minister's power to enter into
arrangements under section 77, including regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting how they are to be entered into;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the circumstances under which they may be
  entered into;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting their contents; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} varying or excluding any provision of this Act or a
  regulation made under it as that provision applies to the subject
  matter of the arrangement.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inconsistencies

**[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** In the event of an
inconsistency between an arrangement referred to in section 76 and an
arrangement referred to in section 77, the arrangement referred to in
section 77 prevails to the extent of the inconsistency.

### [Advice]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162097 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Study and review

**[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator must study
and keep under review matters relating to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the exploration for, and the production, recovery,
  manufacture, processing, transmission, transportation, distribution,
  sale, purchase, lease, exchange and alienation of energy and sources
  of energy in and outside Canada; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the safety and security of regulated facilities and
  abandoned facilities.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reports and recommendations to Minister

**[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator may submit
to the Minister reports on the matters referred to in section 80 from
time to time and may recommend to the Minister any measures within
Parliament's jurisdiction that the Regulator considers to be necessary
or in the public interest for

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the control, supervision, conservation, use,
  marketing and development of energy and sources of energy; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the safety and security of regulated facilities and
  abandoned facilities.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendations on cooperative measures

**[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator may ---
with respect to energy matters, sources of energy and the safety and
security of regulated facilities and abandoned facilities --- recommend
to the Minister any measures that the Regulator considers necessary or
appropriate for cooperation with governmental or other agencies in or
outside Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request of Minister

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator must --- with respect to energy matters, sources of energy
  and the safety and security of regulated facilities and abandoned
  facilities --- provide to the Minister any advice that the Minister
  requests, including advice relating to the export pricing of oil and
  gas, and prepare any study or report that the Minister requests.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition on publication

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator must not publish any advice, study or
  report referred to in subsection (1) without the Minister's consent.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other requests

**[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator may, on
request, provide advice about energy matters, sources of energy and the
safety and security of regulated facilities and abandoned facilities to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ministers, officers and employees of any government
  department or ministry, whether federal, provincial or territorial;
  and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} members, officers and employees of any government
  agency, whether federal, provincial or territorial.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers --- [Inquiries
Act](/eng/acts/I-11)

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 80 to 84, the Regulator has all the powers of commissioners
under Part I of the [Inquiries Act](/eng/acts/I-11).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of government agencies

**[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** In exercising its powers
and performing its duties and functions under sections 80 to 84, the
