Regulator must, to the extent that it is possible to do so, utilize
agencies of the Government of Canada to obtain technical, economic and
statistical information and advice.

### [Cost Recovery]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162121 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator may, with the approval of the Treasury Board, make
  regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} providing for fees, levies or charges that are
    payable for the purpose of recovering all or a portion of any costs
    that the Regulator considers to be attributable to the carrying out
    of its mandate, including costs related to applications that are
    denied or withdrawn; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} providing for the manner of calculating those fees,
    levies or charges and their payment to the Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest on late payments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under subsection (1) may specify
  the rate of interest or the manner of calculating the rate of interest
  payable on any fee, levy or charge that is not paid on or before the
  date on which it is due and may specify the time from which interest
  is payable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debt due to Her Majesty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Fees, levies or charges imposed under this section
  and any interest payable on them constitute a debt to Her Majesty in
  right of Canada and may be recovered as such in any court of competent
  jurisdiction.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

**[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** The [Service Fees
Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does not apply to the fees, levies and charges
referred to in section 87.

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162133 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[certified
document]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[certified document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
  document purporting to be certified by an employee of the Regulator
  who is authorized by the Chief Executive Officer to certify documents
  for the purposes of this section and sealed with the Regulator's seal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of documents --- true copies

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In any action or proceedings, a certified document
  that states that it is a true copy of all or any part of another
  document --- including a minute, decision, document of authorization,
  order, instruction, book of reference, book entry, plan or drawing ---
  is, without proof of the signature or of the official character of the
  person appearing to have signed the certified document, evidence

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} of the original document, or the part of it, that
    it purports to be a copy of;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the original document, or the part of it, was
    made, given or issued under the authority of or deposited with the
    person named in it and was made, given, issued or deposited at the
    time stated in the certified copy, if a time is stated in it; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that the original document, or the part of it, was
    signed, certified, attested or executed by the persons and in the
    manner shown in the certified copy.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of documents --- documents issued
  by Regulator

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In any action or proceedings, a certified document
  that states that a valid and subsisting document has or has not been
  issued by the Regulator to a person named in the certified document is
  evidence of the facts stated in it without proof of the signature or
  official character of the person appearing to have signed the document
  and without further proof.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation

- **[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Directors and commissioners are
  considered to be employees for the purposes of the [Government
  Employees Compensation Act](/eng/acts/G-5) and to be employed in the
  federal public administration for the purposes of any regulations made
  under section 9 of the [Aeronautics Act](/eng/acts/A-2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the Chief Executive Officer
  and employees of the Regulator are employees for the purposes of the
  [Government Employees Compensation Act](/eng/acts/G-5) and are
  employed in the federal public administration for the purposes of any
  regulations made under section 9 of the [Aeronautics
  Act](/eng/acts/A-2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Public Service Superannuation
Act](/eng/acts/P-36)

- **[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Full-time commissioners are
  considered to be persons employed in the public service for the
  purposes of the [Public Service Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/P-36).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, directors and part-time
  commissioners are not persons employed in the public service for the
  purposes of the [Public Service Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/P-36).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the Chief Executive Officer
  and employees of the Regulator are persons employed in the public
  service for the purposes of the [Public Service Superannuation
  Act](/eng/acts/P-36).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** An order made or a
direction given by the Commission, a designated officer or an inspection
officer under this Act is not a [[statutory
instrument]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1) of the
[Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22) if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the order or direction applies to a single person or
  entity;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the order or direction applies with respect to a
  single regulated facility or abandoned facility; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Commission, designated officer or inspection
  officer considers that the order or direction is required immediately
  for the safety or security of persons, regulated facilities or
  abandoned facilities or for the protection of property or the
  environment.

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[Safety, Security and Protection of Persons, Property and Environment]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1162161 .Part}

### [Definitions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162162 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in this Part.

[[compensable damage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[compensable damage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the costs, losses
    and damages for which the Tribunal may award
    compensation. ([dommages indemnisables]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[holder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[holder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the holder of a certificate or permit issued
      under Part 3 or 4 in respect of a regulated facility;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person, company or other entity that is the
      subject of an order made under section 214 in respect of a
      pipeline;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person, company or other entity authorized to
      operate a pipeline under a Special Act;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a company that has been granted leave under Part
      3 to abandon a pipeline;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a person, company or other entity that has been
      granted leave under Part 4 to abandon an international power line
      or interprovincial power line; or

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the holder of an authorization issued under Part
      5 in respect of a work or activity. ([titulaire]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[Tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Tribunal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a pipeline claims tribunal
    established under subsection 143(1). ([Tribunal]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

### [General Duty]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162174 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonable care

**[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** The holder must take all
reasonable care to ensure the safety and security of persons, the safety
and security of regulated facilities and abandoned facilities and the
protection of property and the environment.

### [Regulation of Construction, Operation and Abandonment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162177 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} To
  promote the safety and security of the operation of a regulated
  facility, the Commission may, by order, direct the holder to repair,
  reconstruct or alter part of the regulated facility and direct that,
  until the work is done, that part of the regulated facility not be
  used or be used in accordance with any conditions specified by the
  Commission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other measures

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commission may, by order, direct any of the
  following persons or bodies to take measures in respect of a regulated
  facility, an abandoned facility or any work or activity authorized
  under Part 5 that the Commission considers necessary for the safety
  and security of persons, the safety and security of the regulated
  facility or abandoned facility or the protection of property or the
  environment:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an Indigenous governing body;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the holder or any other person;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the federal government or a federal Crown
    corporation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a provincial government or a provincial Crown
    corporation;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a local authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measures to be taken

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a person or body does not comply with an order
  under subsection (2) or an order under section 109 in relation to an
  abandoned facility or to the abandonment of a pipeline, an offshore
  power line or any facility, equipment or system that is related to an
  offshore renewable energy project, the Commission may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} take any action or measure the Commission considers
    necessary;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorize an officer or employee, or class of
    officers or employees, of the Regulator to take the action or
    measure; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorize a third party to take the action or
    measure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No liability

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No action lies against the Regulator or an officer or
  employee of the Regulator or against a
  [[servant]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} of the
  [[Crown]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as those terms are defined in section
  2 of the [Crown Liability and Proceedings Act](/eng/acts/C-50), in
  respect of any act or omission committed in taking an action or
  measure referred to in subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Third party liability

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A third party that is authorized under subsection (3)
  to take an action or measure is not liable in respect of any act or
  omission committed in good faith in taking the action or measure.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator may, with
the approval of the Governor in Council, make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting the design, construction and operation of
  pipelines, international power lines and interprovincial power lines
  designated by an order made under section 261;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the abandonment of pipelines;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting surveillance or monitoring measures that,
  in relation to the activities referred to in paragraphs (a) and (b),
  are for the safety and security of persons and for the protection of
  property and the environment;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting abandoned facilities;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} requiring holders to have management systems in place
  and to comply with them; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} providing for the elements to be included in those
  management systems --- including human or organizational factors ---
  and respecting any criteria to be met by those management systems.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption orders

**[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** The Commission may make
orders exempting holders, on any conditions that the Commission
considers appropriate, from any or all provisions of a regulation made
under section 96 or 312, in order to, among other things, ensure the
safety and security of persons, the safety and security of regulated
facilities or abandoned facilities or the protection of property or the
environment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** Without limiting the
scope of the regulations that the Regulator may make under section 96,
the Governor in Council may make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} specifying requirements with respect to the
  monitoring of regulated facilities; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the actions or measures to be taken in
  preparation for, or in the case of, an unintended or uncontrolled
  release of oil, gas or any other commodity from a pipeline.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

- **[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  person who contravenes an order made under subsection 95(1) or (2) or
  a regulation made under section 96 is guilty of an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine of not more
    than \$1,000,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than five
    years or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine of not more than
    \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than one year or
    to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsections 379(2) to
  (6)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 379(2) to (6) apply, with any
  modifications that the circumstances require, to an offence referred
  to in subsection (1).

### [Security of Regulated Facilities]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162218 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security regulations

- **[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator may, with the approval of the Governor in Council, make
  regulations respecting the security of regulated facilities, including
  regulations respecting standards, plans and audits that are related to
  the security of regulated facilities and respecting cybersecurity
  matters that are related to regulated facilities and may, for that
  purpose, define what constitutes cybersecurity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes any of the regulations
  made under subsection (1) is guilty of an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine of not more
    than \$500,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than five
    years or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine of not more than
    \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than one year or
    to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsections 379(2) to
  (6)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 379(2) to (6) apply, with any
  modifications that the circumstances require, to the offence referred
  to in subsection (2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abandoned facilities

- **[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not make contact with, alter or remove an abandoned
  facility unless they are authorized to do so in an order made under
  subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A designated officer may make an order, subject to
  any conditions that the designated officer considers appropriate,
  authorizing a person to make contact with, alter or remove an
  abandoned facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may make regulations respecting the
  circumstances in which or conditions under which an order under
  subsection (2) is not necessary and the Commission may make orders
  respecting the circumstances in which or conditions under which an
  order under subsection (2) is not necessary.

### [Administration and Enforcement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162235 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation of inspection officers

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief Executive Officer may designate any person or member of any
  class of persons as an inspection officer to administer and enforce
  this Part, Parts 3 to 5 and section 335.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purposes of designation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The purposes for which an inspection officer may be
  designated are:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to ensure the safety and security of persons;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to ensure the safety and security of regulated
    facilities and abandoned facilities; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to ensure the protection of property and the
    environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator must provide every inspection officer
  designated under subsection (1) with a certificate of authority. That
  inspection officer must, if so requested, produce the certificate to
  the occupant or person in charge of a place referred to in subsection
  103(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to enter

- **[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  inspection officer may, for a purpose related to verifying compliance
  or preventing non-compliance with this Part, any of Parts 3 to 5 or
  section 335, or for a purpose referred to in subsection 102(2), enter
  a place in which the officer has reasonable grounds to believe that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is anything to which this Part or any of
    Parts 3 to 5 applies, including a regulated facility or an abandoned
    facility;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there has been carried on, is being carried on or
    is likely to be carried on any activity in respect of which this
    Part, any of Parts 3 to 5 or section 335 applies; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} there is any book, record, electronic data or other
    document relating to the application of this Part, any of Parts 3 to
    5 or section 335.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers on entry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The inspection officer may, for that purpose,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} examine anything in the place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use any means of communication in the place or
    cause it to be used;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} use any computer system in the place, or cause it
    to be used, to examine data contained in or available to it;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} prepare a document, or cause one to be prepared,
    based on the data;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} make copies of any information contained in any
    books, records, electronic data or other documents or in computer
    systems and use any copying equipment in the place, or cause it to
    be used to make copies of the information;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} conduct tests or analyses of anything in the place;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} take measurements or samples of anything in the
    place;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} take photographs or make recordings or sketches of
    anything in the place;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} remove anything from the place for examination or
    copying;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} direct any person to put any machinery, vehicle or
    equipment in the place into operation or to cease operating it;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} prohibit or limit access to all or part of the
    place or to anything in the place;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} direct the owner or person in charge of the place
    or a person at the place to establish their identity to the
    inspection officer's satisfaction or to stop or start an activity;
    or

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} direct the owner or a person having possession,
    care or control of anything in the place to not move it, or to
    restrict its movement, for as long as the inspection officer
    considers necessary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance audit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the powers set out in
  subsection (2) include the power to conduct a compliance audit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to assist

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The owner or person in charge of the place and every
  person in the place must give all assistance that is reasonably
  required to enable the inspection officer to perform their duties and
  functions under this section and must provide any documents or
  information, and access to any data, that are reasonably required for
  that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons accompanying

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The inspection officer may be accompanied by any
  other person that they believe is necessary to help them perform their
  duties and functions under this section, and the person is not liable
  for doing so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entry on private property

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The inspection officer and any person accompanying
  them may enter private property --- other than a dwelling-house or
  living quarters --- and pass through it in order to gain entry to a
  place referred to in subsection (1) and the person is not liable for
  doing so. For greater certainty, no person has a right to object to
  that use of the property and no warrant is required for the entry,
  unless the property is a dwelling-house or living quarters.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warrant to enter dwelling-house or living
quarters

- **[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the place is a dwelling-house or living quarters, the inspection
  officer may enter it without the occupant's consent only under the
  authority of a warrant issued under subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to issue warrant

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application, a
  justice of the peace may issue a warrant authorizing the inspection
  officer to enter a dwelling-house or living quarters, subject to any
  conditions specified in the warrant, if the justice is satisfied by
  information on oath that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the dwelling-house or living quarters is a place
    referred to in subsection 103(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} entry to the dwelling-house or living quarters is
    necessary for a purpose related to verifying compliance or
    preventing non-compliance with this Part, any of Parts 3 to 5 or
    section 335, or for a purpose referred to in subsection 102(2); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} entry was refused by the occupant or there are
    reasonable grounds to believe that entry will be refused by, or that
    consent to entry cannot be obtained from, the occupant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of force

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In executing a warrant to enter a dwelling-house or
  living quarters, an inspection officer may use force only if the use
  of force has been specifically authorized in the warrant and they are
  accompanied by a peace officer.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

**[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** No action or other
proceeding of a civil nature lies against an inspection officer in
respect of anything that is done or omitted to be done in good faith
while exercising their powers or performing their duties and functions
under this Part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- obstruction

**[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** It is prohibited to
obstruct or hinder an inspection officer who is exercising their powers
or performing their duties and functions under this Part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- false statements or
information

**[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** It is prohibited to
knowingly make a false or misleading statement or knowingly provide
false or misleading information in connection with any matter under this
Part, any of Parts 3 to 5 or section 335 to any inspection officer who
is exercising their powers or performing their duties and functions
under this Part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of non-compliance

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an inspection officer has reasonable grounds to believe that a person
  has contravened this Part, any of Parts 3 to 5 or section 335, he or
  she may issue a notice of non-compliance to the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice must be made in writing and must set out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person to whom the notice is
    directed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the provision of this Part, any of Parts 3 to 5 or
    section 335 or any of their regulations that is alleged to have been
    contravened, the order or decision that is alleged to have been
    contravened, or the condition of any certificate, order or decision,
    permit or authorization, leave or exemption that is alleged to have
    been contravened;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the relevant facts surrounding the alleged
    contravention; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the period within which the person may provide
    comments in response to the notice.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Grounds for making order

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an inspection officer who is expressly authorized by the Chief
  Executive Officer to make orders under this section has reasonable
  grounds to believe that there is or is likely to be a contravention of
  this Part or any of Parts 3 to 5 or section 335, or for a purpose
  referred to in subsection 102(2), the inspection officer may, by
  order, direct a person to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} stop doing something that is in contravention of
    this Part or any of Parts 3 to 5 or section 335 or cause it to be
    stopped;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} take any measure that is necessary in order to
    comply with this Part or any of Parts 3 to 5 or section 335 or
    mitigate the effects of non-compliance;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} stop doing something that may cause a hazard to the
    safety or security of persons, or damage to property or the
    environment or cause it to be stopped; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} take any measure that is necessary to prevent or
    mitigate the hazard to the safety or security of persons or damage
    to property or the environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The order may require work associated with a
  facility, including a regulated facility, abandoned facility or ground
  disturbance to be suspended until the hazardous or detrimental
  situation has been remedied to the satisfaction of an inspection
  officer or the order is stayed or rescinded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice and report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An inspection officer who makes an order under this
  section must, as soon as possible,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give written notice of the order to the person to
    whom it is directed, including the terms of the order and a
    statement of the reasons for the order; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} report the circumstances and terms of the order to
    the Commission.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of appeal

**[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
an appeal before the Commission from an order made under section 109
does not operate as a stay of the order, but the Commission may grant a
stay pending the outcome of the appeal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information confidential

**[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** An inspection officer
must not disclose to any person any information regarding any secret
process or trade secret obtained while performing duties and functions
under this Part, except for the purposes of this Part or as required by
law.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment --- duty to assist
and orders

- **[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes
  subsection 103(4) or fails to comply with an order under section 109
  is guilty of an offence and is liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine of not more
    than \$1,000,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than five
    years or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine of not more than
    \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than one year or
    to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence --- no notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person must not be found guilty of an offence for
  failing to comply with an order under section 109 unless the person
  was given written notice of the order in accordance with paragraph
  109(3)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment --- obstruction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes section 106 is guilty of
  an offence punishable on summary conviction and is liable, for a first
  offence, to a fine of not more than \$100,000 and, for any subsequent
  offence, to a fine of not more than \$300,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsections 379(2) to
  (6)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 379(2) to (6) apply, with any
  modifications that the circumstances require, in respect of an offence
  under subsections (1) and (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment --- false
  statements or information

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes section 107 is guilty of
  an offence punishable on summary conviction and is liable to a fine of
  not more than \$300,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsections 379(3) to
  (6)

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 379(3) to (6) apply, with any
  modifications that the circumstances require, in respect of an offence
  under subsection (5).

### [Privilege]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162332 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in this section.

  [[coroner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[coroner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes any medical examiner or
      other person carrying out the duties and functions of a
      coroner. ([coroner]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[recording]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[recording]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the whole or any part of
      either a recording of voice communications originating from, or
      received on or in, a regulated facility, or a video recording of
      the activities of the operating personnel of the regulated
      facility, which recording of voice communications or video
      recording is made, using recording equipment to which the
      operating personnel does not have access, in a place where
      operations of the facility are carried out and includes a
      transcript or substantial summary of such a
      recording. ([enregistrement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Privilege for recordings

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every recording is privileged and, except as provided
  by this section, a person, including any person to whom access is
  provided under this section, must not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} knowingly communicate a recording or permit it to
    be communicated to any person; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be required to produce a recording or give evidence
    relating to it in any legal, disciplinary or other proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access by Regulator

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any recording that relates to an accident that is the
  subject of an inquiry under subsection 32(2) must be released to the
  Regulator on request for the purposes of the inquiry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use by Regulator

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may make any use of any recording
  obtained under this Act that it considers necessary for the purposes
  of the inquiry under subsection 32(2), but, subject to subsection (5),
  must not knowingly communicate or permit to be communicated to anyone
  any portion of it that is unrelated to the inquiry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access by peace officers, coroners and
  other investigators

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator must make available any recording
  obtained under this Act to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a coroner who requests access to it for the purpose
    of an investigation that the coroner is conducting;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person carrying out an inquiry under subsection
    32(2); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any person carrying out a coordinated investigation
    referred to in the [Canadian Transportation Accident Investigation
    and Safety Board Act](/eng/acts/C-23.4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of court or coroner

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other provision of this section, if, in
  any proceedings before a court or coroner, a request for the
  production and discovery of a recording is made, the court or coroner
  must cause notice of the request to be given to the Regulator, if the
  Regulator is not a party to the proceedings and, [in
  camera]{.otherLang lang="la"}, examine the recording and give the
  Regulator a reasonable opportunity to make representations with
  respect to it. If the court or coroner concludes in the circumstances
  of the case that the public interest in the proper administration of
  justice outweighs in importance the privilege attached to the
  recording by virtue of this section, the court or coroner must order
  the production and discovery of the recording, subject to any
  restrictions or conditions that the court or coroner considers
  appropriate, and may require any person to give evidence that relates
  to the recording.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use prohibited

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A recording is not to be used against agents or
  employees who are directly or indirectly involved in the operation of
  a regulated facility in disciplinary proceedings, in proceedings
  relating to their capacity or competence to perform their functions,
  or in legal or other proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (6),
  [[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes any person appointed or
  designated to conduct a public inquiry into a [[pipeline
  occurrence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, within the meaning of section 2 of
  the [Canadian Transportation Accident Investigation and Safety Board
  Act](/eng/acts/C-23.4), under the [Inquiries Act](/eng/acts/I-11).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voluntary reporting

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator may, subject to the approval of the Governor in Council,
  make regulations for the establishment and administration of systems
  for the voluntary reporting to the Regulator of alleged non-compliance
  with this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use by Regulator

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may, subject to this section, make any
  use of any report made to it in accordance with regulations made under
  subsection (1) that it considers necessary for the purposes of this
  Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules for protection of identity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under subsection (1) may include
  rules for the protection of the identity of persons who make a report.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain information privileged

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the identity of a person who has made a report
  to the Regulator in accordance with regulations made under
  subsection (1) is protected by rules referred to in subsection (3),
  information that could reasonably be expected to reveal that identity
  is privileged, and a person must not

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} knowingly communicate it or permit it to be
    communicated to any person; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be required to produce it or give evidence relating
    to it in any legal, disciplinary or other proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use prohibited

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A report made to the Regulator under a voluntary
  reporting system established by regulations made under subsection (1)
  must not be used against the person who made the report in any legal,
  disciplinary or other proceedings if the person's identity is
  protected by rules referred to in subsection (3).

### [Administrative Monetary Penalties]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162370 .Subheading}

#### [Regulator's Powers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162371 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator may, with the approval of the Governor in Council, make
  regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designating as a violation that may be proceeded
    with in accordance with this Act

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the contravention of any specified provision of
      this Act or the regulations,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the contravention of any order or decision, or
      of any order or decision of any specified class of orders or
      decisions, made under this Act, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the failure to comply with any condition of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} any certificate, licence, permit or
        authorization, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} any leave or exemption granted under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the determination of or the method of
    determining the amount payable as the penalty, which may be
    different for individuals and other persons, for each violation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the service of documents required or
    authorized under section 120, 125 or 128, including the manner and
    proof of service and the circumstances under which documents are
    considered to be served.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum amount of penalty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The amount that may be determined under any
  regulations made under paragraph (1)(b) as the penalty for a violation
  must not be more than \$25,000, in the case of an individual, and
  \$100,000, in the case of any other person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Regulator

- **[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish the form of notices of violation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} establish, in respect of each violation, a
    short-form description to be used in notices of violation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Commission

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Commission may designate persons or classes of
  persons to conduct reviews under section 128.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Chief Executive Officer

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Executive Officer may designate persons or
  classes of persons who are authorized to issue notices of violation.

#### [Violations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162394 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commission of violation

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes or
  fails to comply with a provision, order, decision or condition
  designated under paragraph 115(1)(a) commits a violation and is liable
  to a penalty of an amount to be determined in accordance with the
  regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of penalty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The purpose of the penalty is to promote compliance
  with this Act and not to punish.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability of directors, officers, etc.

**[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** If a corporation
commits a violation, any director, officer or agent or mandatary of the
corporation who directed, authorized, assented to, acquiesced in or
participated in the commission of the violation is a party to the
violation and is liable to a penalty of an amount to be determined in
accordance with the regulations, whether or not the corporation has been
proceeded against in accordance with this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of violation

**[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** In any proceedings
under this Act against a person in relation to a violation, it is
sufficient proof of the violation to establish that it was committed by
an employee or agent or mandatary of the person, whether or not the
employee or agent or mandatary is identified or proceeded against in
accordance with this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance and service of notice of
violation

- **[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person designated under subsection 116(2) has reasonable grounds to
  believe that a person has committed a violation, the designated person
  may issue a notice of violation and cause it to be served on the
  person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice of violation must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} name the person that is believed to have committed
    the violation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} set out the relevant facts surrounding the
    violation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the amount of the penalty for the
    violation;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} inform the person of their right to request a
    review with respect to the violation or the amount of the penalty,
    and of the period referred to in section 125 within which that right
    must be exercised;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} inform the person of the manner of paying the
    penalty set out in the notice; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} inform the person that, if they do not pay the
    penalty or exercise their right referred to in paragraph (d), they
    will be considered to have committed the violation and that they are
    liable to the penalty set out in the notice.

#### [Rules about Violations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162415 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain defences not available

- **[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person named in a notice of violation does not have a defence by
  reason that the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} exercised due diligence to prevent the commission
    of the violation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reasonably and honestly believed in the existence
    of facts that, if true, would exonerate the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Common law principles

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every rule and principle of the common law that makes
  any circumstance a justification or excuse in relation to a charge for
  an offence under this Act applies in respect of a violation to the
  extent that it is not inconsistent with this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing violation

**[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** A violation that is
committed or continued on more than one day constitutes a separate
violation for each day on which it is committed or continued.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Violation or offence

- **[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Proceeding with any act or omission
  as a violation under this Act precludes proceeding with it as an
  offence under this Act, and proceeding with it as an offence under
  this Act precludes proceeding with it as a violation under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Violations not offences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a violation is not an offence
  and, accordingly, section 126 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
  does not apply in respect of a violation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period or prescription

**[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** No notice of violation
in respect of a violation may be issued more than two years from the day
on which the subject matter of the violation arises.

#### [Reviews]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162432 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to request review

**[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** A person who is served
with a notice of violation may, within 30 days after the day on which it
is served, or within any longer period that the Commission allows, make
a request to the Commission for a review of the amount of the penalty or
the facts of the violation, or both.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Correction or cancellation of notice of
violation

**[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** At any time before a
request for a review in respect of a notice of violation is received by
the Commission, a person designated under subsection 116(2) may cancel
the notice of violation or correct an error in it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

- **[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  receipt of a request made under section 125, the Commission must
  conduct the review or cause the review to be conducted by a person
  designated under subsection 116(1.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commission must conduct the review if the notice
  of violation was issued by a person designated under subsection
  116(1.1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Object of review

- **[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commission or the person conducting the review must determine, as the
  case may be, whether the amount of the penalty for the violation was
  determined in accordance with the regulations or whether the person
  committed the violation, or both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commission or the person conducting the review
  must make a determination in writing and cause the person who
  requested the review to be served with a copy of the determination and
  the reasons for it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Correction of penalty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Commission or the person conducting the review
  determines that the amount of the penalty for the violation was not
  determined in accordance with the regulations, the Commission or the
  person, as the case may be, must correct the amount of the penalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibility

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Commission or the person conducting the review
  determines that the person who requested the review committed the
  violation, the person who requested the review is liable to the
  penalty as set out in the determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination final

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection 69(1) and (2), a determination
  made under this section is final and binding and, except for judicial
  review under the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7), is not subject
  to appeal or to review by any court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Federal Court

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 28 of the [Federal Courts
  Act](/eng/acts/F-7), the Federal Court has exclusive original
  jurisdiction to hear and determine an application for judicial review
  of a determination made under this section by the Commission.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

**[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** If the facts of a
violation are reviewed, the person who issued the notice of violation
must establish, on a balance of probabilities, that the person named in
it committed the violation identified in it.

#### [Responsibility]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162457 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment

**[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130 .sectionLabel}** If a person pays the
penalty set out in a notice of violation, the person is considered to
have committed the violation and proceedings in respect of it are ended.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to act

**[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** A person that neither
pays the penalty imposed under this Act nor requests a review within the
period referred to in section 125 is considered to have committed the
violation and is liable to the penalty.

#### [Recovery of Penalties]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162462 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debts to Her Majesty

- **[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  penalty constitutes a debt due to Her Majesty in right of Canada and
  may be recovered as such in the Federal Court or any other court of
  competent jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period or prescription

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Proceedings to recover the debt may be instituted no
  later than the fifth anniversary of the day on which the debt becomes
  payable.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commission may issue a certificate of non-payment certifying the
  unpaid amount of any debt referred to in subsection 132(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration in Federal Court

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Registration in the Federal Court or in any other
  court of competent jurisdiction of a certificate of non-payment issued
  under subsection (1) has the same effect as a judgment of that court
  for a debt of the amount specified in the certificate and all related
  registration costs.

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162473 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissibility of documents

**[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** In the absence of
evidence to the contrary, a document that appears to be a notice issued
under subsection 120(1) is presumed to be authentic and is proof of its
contents in any proceeding in respect of a violation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

**[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator may make
public the nature of a violation, the name of the person who committed
it and the amount of the penalty.

### [Polluter Pays Principle]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162478 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose

**[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** The purpose of sections
137 to 142 is to reinforce the "polluter pays" principle by, among other
things, imposing financial requirements on any company that is
authorized under this Act to construct or operate a pipeline.

### [Liability]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162481 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of loss, damage, costs, expenses

- **[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an unintended or uncontrolled release from a pipeline of oil, gas or
  any other commodity occurs, all persons to whose fault or negligence
  the release is attributable or who are by law responsible for others
  to whose fault or negligence the release is attributable are jointly
  and severally, or solidarily, liable for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all actual loss or damage incurred by any person as
    a result of the release or as a result of any action or measure
    taken in relation to the release;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the costs and expenses reasonably incurred by Her
    Majesty in right of Canada or a province, any Indigenous governing
    body or any other person in taking any action or measure in relation
    to the release; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all loss of non-use value relating to a public
    resource that is affected by the release or by any action or measure
    taken in relation to the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contribution based on degree of fault

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The persons who are at fault or negligent or who are
  by law responsible for persons who are at fault or negligent are
  liable to make contributions to each other or to indemnify each other
  in the degree to which they are respectively at fault or negligent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vicarious liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The company that is authorized under this Act to
  construct or operate the pipeline from which the release occurred is
  jointly and severally, or solidarily, liable with any contractor ---
  to whose fault or negligence the release is attributable --- that
  performs work for the company for the actual loss or damage, the costs
  and expenses and the loss of non-use value, described in paragraphs
  (1)(a) to (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absolute liability

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If an unintended or uncontrolled release of oil, gas
  or any other commodity from a pipeline occurs, the company that is
  authorized under this Act to construct or operate that pipeline is
  liable, without proof of fault or negligence, up to the applicable
  limit of liability that is set out in subsection (5) for the actual
  loss or damage, the costs and expenses and the loss of non-use value,
  described in paragraphs (1)(a) to (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limits of liability

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (4), the limits of
  liability are,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a company that is authorized under
    this Act to construct or operate one or more pipelines that
    individually or in the aggregate have the capacity to transport at
    least 250,000 barrels of oil per day, \$1 billion or, if a greater
    amount is prescribed by the regulations, that amount; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a company that is authorized under
    this Act to construct or operate any other pipeline, the amount
    prescribed by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations --- limits of liability

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by regulation, on the
  Minister's recommendation,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribe an amount greater than \$1 billion for
    the purposes of paragraph (5)(a); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribe an amount for the purposes of paragraph
    (5)(b) in respect of a company or class of companies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability under another law ---
  subsection (4)

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If a company is liable under subsection (4) with
  respect to a release and it is also liable under any other Act,
  without proof of fault or negligence, for that release, the company is
  liable up to the greater of the limit of liability referred to in
  subsection (5) that applies to it and the limit up to which it is
  liable under the other Act. If the other Act does not set out a limit
  of liability, the limits referred to in subsection (5) do not apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs and expenses not recoverable
  under [Fisheries Act](/eng/acts/F-14)

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The costs and expenses that are recoverable by Her
  Majesty in right of Canada or a province under this section are not
  recoverable under subsection 42(1) of the [Fisheries
  Act](/eng/acts/F-14).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceedings --- loss of non-use value

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Only Her Majesty in right of Canada or a province may
  institute proceedings to recover a loss of non-use value described in
  paragraph (1)(c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Claims

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} All claims under this section may be sued for and
  recovered in any court of competent jurisdiction in Canada. Claims in
  favour of persons incurring actual loss or damage described in
  paragraph (1)(a) are to be distributed pro rata and rank in priority
  over claims for costs and expenses described in paragraph (1)(b), and
  the claims for costs and expenses rank in priority over claims to
  recover a loss of non-use value described in paragraph (1)(c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (8) and (9), nothing in this
  section suspends or limits

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any legal liability or remedy for an act or
    omission by reason only that the act or omission is an offence under
    this Act or gives rise to liability under this section;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any recourse, indemnity or relief available at law
    to a person who is liable under this section against any other
    person; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the operation of any applicable law or rule of law
    that is consistent with this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period or prescription

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} Proceedings in respect of claims under this section
  may be instituted no later than the third anniversary of the day on
  which the loss, damage or costs and expenses were incurred but in no
  case after the sixth anniversary of the day on which the release
  occurred.

### [Financial Requirements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162517 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Financial resources

- **[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  company that is authorized under this Act to construct or operate a
  pipeline must maintain the amount of financial resources necessary to
  pay the amount of the limit of liability referred to in subsection
  137(5) that applies to it or, if the Commission specifies a greater
  amount, that amount.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Types of financial resources

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the regulations made under subsection (7),
  the Commission may, by order, direct a company, either individually or
  as a member of a class of companies authorized under this Act to
  construct or operate a pipeline, to maintain the amount of financial
  resources that is referred to in subsection (1) of the types of
  financial resources that the Commission specifies, including types
  that must be readily accessible to the company and, if the Commission
  specifies types of financial resources, it may specify the amount that
  the company is required to maintain of each type.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to satisfy Commission

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} At the Commission's request, the company must satisfy
  the Commission that it meets the requirement to maintain the amount of
  financial resources referred to in subsection (1) and that it complies
  with any order made under subsection (2). The Commission may consider,
  among other things, the company's financial statements, letters of
  credit, guarantees, bonds or suretyships and insurance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Greater amount

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Commission may, by order, specify an amount for
  the purposes of subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loss of non-use value

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} When the Commission specifies an amount for the
  purposes of subsection (1), it is not required to consider any
  potential loss of non-use value relating to a public resource that is
  affected by an unintended or uncontrolled release of oil, gas or any
  other commodity from a pipeline or by any action or measure taken in
  relation to the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing obligation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A company that is authorized under this Act to
  construct or operate a pipeline must maintain the amount of financial
  resources referred to in subsection (1) and must comply with any order
  made under subsection (2) and any regulation made under subsection (7)
  only until it obtains leave from the Commission to abandon the
  operation of its pipeline.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the Minister's
  recommendation, make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} providing for the types of financial resources from
    which the Commission may choose if it specifies types of financial
    resources under subsection (2), including those from which the
    Commission may choose if it specifies types of financial resources
    that must be readily accessible to the company; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the amount of financial resources that
    must be readily accessible to a company individually or as a member
    of a class of companies.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pooled fund

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a company
  may meet all or a portion of its financial requirements under
  subsection 138(1) by participating in a pooled fund that is
  established by companies authorized under this Act to construct or
  operate a pipeline, that meets the requirements established by the
  regulations and from which the Regulator may withdraw any amount under
  subsection 141(6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Difference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a company meets only a portion of its financial
  requirements by participating in a pooled fund and the amount of funds
  in the pooled fund to which it has access --- in the event that there
  is an unintended or uncontrolled release of oil, gas or any other
  commodity from a pipeline that the company is authorized under this
  Act to construct or operate --- is less than the amount of financial
  resources referred to in subsection 138(1), the company must maintain
  an amount equal to the difference between those amounts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the Minister's
  recommendation, make regulations respecting a pooled fund referred to
  in subsection (1), including regulations specifying

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the minimum amount of the pooled fund and the
    minimum amount of the pooled fund that must be readily accessible;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any condition that a company must meet in order to
    participate in the pooled fund, including the minimum amount that a
    company must contribute to the pooled fund;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the maximum amount that a company may withdraw from
    the pooled fund; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the maximum portion of a company's financial
    requirements that may be met by participating in the pooled fund.

### [Reimbursement by Company]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162546 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reimbursement ---  measures taken by
government institution

**[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** If an unintended or
uncontrolled release of oil, gas or any other commodity from a pipeline
occurs, the Commission may, by order, direct the company that is
authorized under this Act to construct or operate that pipeline to
reimburse any federal, provincial or municipal government institution,
any Indigenous governing body or any person for the costs and expenses
that the Commission considers reasonable that they incurred in taking
any reasonable action or measure in relation to the release, even if the
costs and expenses are more than the amount of the limit of liability
referred to in subsection 137(5) that applies to that company.

### [Designated Company]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162549 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

- **[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the Minister's recommendation, designate
  any company that is authorized under this Act to construct or operate
  a pipeline from which an unintended or uncontrolled release of oil,
  gas or any other commodity has occurred if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Governor in Council considers that the company
    does not have or is not likely to have the financial resources
    necessary to pay

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the costs and expenses incurred, or to be
      incurred, in taking any action or measure in relation to the
      release, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the compensation that might be awarded for
      compensable damage caused by the release; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the company does not comply with an order of the
    Commission with respect to any action or measure to be taken in
    relation to the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measures to be taken

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Governor in Council designates a company under
  subsection (1), the Regulator or any of its officers or employees ---
  or class of officers or employees --- that the Regulator authorizes
  may take any action or measure that they consider necessary in
  relation to the release or they may authorize a third party to take
  any action or measure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No action lies against the Regulator or an officer or
  employee of the Regulator or against a
  [[servant]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} of the
  [[Crown]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as those terms are defined in section
  2 of the [Crown Liability and Proceedings Act](/eng/acts/C-50), in
  respect of any act or omission committed in taking any action or
  measure in relation to the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Third party liability

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A third party that is authorized under subsection (2)
  to take any action or measure referred to in that subsection is not
  liable in respect of any act or omission committed in good faith in
  taking the action or measure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reimbursement ---  measures taken by
  government institution

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the Governor in Council designates a company under
  subsection (1), the Regulator may reimburse any federal, provincial or
  municipal government institution, any Indigenous governing body or any
  person the costs and expenses that the Regulator considers reasonable
  that they incurred in taking any reasonable action or measure in
  relation to the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal from pooled fund

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the Governor in Council designates a company under
  subsection (1) and that company participates in a pooled fund
  described in subsection 139(1), the Regulator may withdraw any amount
  from the pooled fund that is necessary to pay the costs and expenses
  incurred in taking any action or measure under subsection (2) and to
  reimburse the costs and expenses referred to in subsection (5).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations imposing fees, etc.

- **[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the Treasury Board's
  approval, the Regulator must, for the purposes of recovering the
  amounts paid out of the Consolidated Revenue Fund under subsection
  171(1), even if those amounts are more than the amount of the limit of
  liability referred to in subsection 137(5) that applies to a
  designated company, make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} imposing fees, levies or charges on a designated
    company and, if the amount recovered from the designated company is
    not sufficient to recover the amounts paid out, imposing fees,
    levies or charges on companies that are authorized under this Act to
    construct or operate pipelines that transport the same commodity or
    a commodity of the same class as the commodity that was released
    from a pipeline constructed or operated by the designated company;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the manner of calculating the fees,
    levies and charges and their payment to the Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest on late payments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A regulation made under subsection (1) may specify
  the rate of interest or the manner of calculating the rate of interest
  payable by a company either individually or as a member of a class of
  companies on any fee, levy or charge not paid by the company on or
  before the date it is due and the time from which interest is payable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debt due to Her Majesty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Fees, levies or charges imposed under this section
  and any interest payable on them constitute a debt that is due to Her
  Majesty in right of Canada and may be recovered as such in any court
  of competent jurisdiction.

### [Pipeline Claims Tribunal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1162576 .Subheading}

#### [Establishment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162577 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment of Tribunal

- **[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, by order, after a designation is made under
  subsection 141(1), establish a pipeline claims tribunal whose purpose
  is to examine and adjudicate, as expeditiously as the circumstances
  and considerations of fairness permit, the claims for compensation
  made under this Act in relation to the release that occurred from the
  designated company's pipeline and specify the location of its head
  office.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, the Governor in Council may establish a
  pipeline claims tribunal only if, having regard to the extent of the
  compensable damage caused by the release, the estimated cost of paying
  compensation in respect of that damage and the advantages of having
  claims dealt with by an administrative tribunal, the Governor in
  Council considers it in the public interest to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Claims treated equitably

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A Tribunal must exercise its powers and perform its
  duties and functions with respect to claims for compensation in an
  equitable manner, without discrimination on the basis of nationality
  or residence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction of courts

**[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
nothing in sections 143 and 145 to 173 is to be construed as restricting
the jurisdiction of a court of competent jurisdiction with respect to
the release referred to in subsection 143(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public notice

**[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** Immediately after a
Tribunal is established, the Regulator must notify the public, in a
manner that the Tribunal considers appropriate, of the Tribunal's
purpose and the manner in which to obtain information on making a claim
for compensation and must publish in the [Canada
Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) a notice setting out that purpose
and the manner in which to obtain that information.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members of Tribunal

- **[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Tribunal is to have at least three members, each of whom is to be
  appointed by the Governor in Council, on the Minister's
  recommendation, to hold office for a term of not more than five years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members' qualifications

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The members of the Tribunal are to be appointed from
  among persons who are retired judges of a superior court or members of
  at least 10 years standing at either the bar of a province or the
  [Chambre des notaires du Québec]{.otherLang lang="fr"}.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Replacing member

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a member is absent or becomes incapacitated,
  resigns or dies, the Governor in Council may appoint a replacement for
  that member.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator must pay the members the remuneration
  and expenses that are fixed by the Governor in Council.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tenure

- **[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each
  member of a Tribunal is to hold office during good behaviour, but may
  be removed by the Governor in Council for cause.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination of Tribunal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may terminate the appointment
  of the members of the Tribunal if the Governor in Council is satisfied
  that the Tribunal has no work to carry out.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

**[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** No action lies against
any member of a Tribunal for anything done or omitted to be done by the
member in the exercise or purported exercise of a power or in the
performance or purported performance of a duty or function of the
Tribunal.

#### [Chairperson and Staff]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162605 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson

- **[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council must, on the Minister's recommendation, designate
  one of the members of a Tribunal to be the Chairperson of the
  Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson's powers, duties and
  functions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson apportions work among the members of
  the Tribunal and, if he or she establishes a panel, assigns members to
  the panel and designates a member to preside over it. The Chairperson
  also has supervision over and direction of the work of the Tribunal's
  staff.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff

**[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** A Tribunal may employ
the staff that it considers necessary for the proper exercise of its
powers and performance of its duties and functions, prescribe their
duties and, subject to any regulations, the conditions of their
employment and, with the Treasury Board's approval, fix their
remuneration.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Technical or specialized knowledge

**[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** A Tribunal may engage,
on a temporary basis, the services of counsel or notaries and other
persons having technical or specialized knowledge to assist it in its
work, prescribe their duties and the conditions of their employment and,
with the Treasury Board's approval, fix their remuneration and expenses.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment by Regulator

**[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator must pay
the remuneration and expenses referred to in sections 150 and 151.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff and facilities

**[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator must
provide a Tribunal with any professional, technical, secretarial,
clerical and other assistance and any facilities and supplies that the
Regulator considers necessary to enable the Tribunal to exercise its
powers and perform its duties and functions.

#### [Tribunal's Powers, Duties and Functions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162619 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearings

**[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** A Tribunal must conduct
its hearings in Canada on the dates and at the times and locations that
it considers appropriate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

- **[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Tribunal has all the powers, rights and privileges vested in a
  superior court with respect to any matters within its jurisdiction,
  including the attendance, swearing and examination of witnesses, the
  production and inspection of documents and the enforcement of its
  orders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence at hearings

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal is not, in the hearing of any matter,
  bound by the legal rules of evidence. However, it must not receive as
  evidence anything that would be inadmissible in a court by reason of
  any privilege under the law of evidence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examinations

**[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
adjudicating a claim for compensation or an application for
reconsideration, a Tribunal may require a claimant or applicant to
undergo medical or other examinations that the Tribunal considers
reasonably necessary.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Frivolous or vexatious claims

**[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** A Tribunal may refuse
to hear any claim for compensation or application for reconsideration
that it considers to be frivolous or vexatious.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written submissions

**[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** A Tribunal may examine
and adjudicate any claim for compensation or application for
reconsideration based only on written submissions.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules

**[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** A Tribunal may make the
rules that it considers necessary for the exercise of its powers and the
performance of its duties and functions, including rules respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} procedures for making a claim for compensation and
  those for making an application for reconsideration;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the information that must be included with a claim
  for compensation or an application for reconsideration;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of its examination of claims for
  compensation and applications for reconsideration, including the
  conduct of its hearings;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the form and manner in which evidence must be
  submitted; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a quorum.

#### [Claims for Compensation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162640 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person, partnership or unincorporated organization or federal,
  provincial or municipal government or any Indigenous governing body
  may, within the prescribed period, make to a Tribunal a claim for
  compensation for compensable damage caused by the release that
  occurred from a designated company's pipeline.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Panel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} As soon as feasible after the day on which the claim
  is made, the Chairperson must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} assign the claim to the Tribunal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} establish a panel of the Tribunal and assign the
    claim to that panel; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} assign the claim to an existing panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson must notify the claimant, the
  designated company and the Regulator that the claim has been assigned.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers, duties and functions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A panel has the powers, other than the power
  described in section 159, and the duties and functions of the Tribunal
  with respect to any claim for compensation that is before that panel.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public hearings

**[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161 .sectionLabel}** Tribunal hearings are
to be held in public. However, a Tribunal may hold all or part of a
hearing in private if it considers that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a public hearing would not be in the public interest;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person's privacy interest outweighs the principle
  that hearings be open to the public; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} confidential business information may be disclosed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim award of compensation

**[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** If it is authorized to
do so by the regulations, a Tribunal may award interim compensation in
respect of a claim for compensation. If the Tribunal awards interim
compensation, it must advise the Regulator, by notice, of the amount
awarded.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determining compensation

- **[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Tribunal must decide whether to award a claimant compensation for the
  compensable damage that is referred to in their claim for
  compensation. If it decides to award compensation, it must, having
  regard to any other amount of compensation that the claimant has been
  paid for that damage, determine the amount of compensation in
  accordance with the regulations and, if it is authorized by the
  regulations to award costs with respect to a claimant's claim for
  compensation and it decides to do so, it must determine the amount of
  those costs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice --- decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal must advise the claimant and the
  designated company, by notice, of its decision with respect to the
  claim for compensation and, if compensation or costs are awarded, it
  must also advise the Regulator by that notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The notice must indicate

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the amount of any compensation or costs determined
    under subsection (1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any reduction in the amount of compensation
    provided for by the regulations; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any amounts that have already been paid to the
    claimant for the compensable damage that is referred to in their
    claim.

#### [Payment by Regulator]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1162670 .Subheading}
