- **[[374]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-374 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  exercising its powers and performing its duties and functions, the
  Regulator must give effect to CUSMA, the CCFTA and the CCRFTA.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, either on the
  recommendation of the Minister made at the request of the Regulator or
  on the Governor in Council's own motion, give directions respecting
  the manner in which the Regulator is to perform the duty imposed on it
  by subsection (1) or the interpretation to be given to CUSMA, the
  CCFTA or the CCRFTA by the Regulator for the purposes of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A direction issued under subsection (2) is binding on
  the Regulator from the time it comes into force and, unless it
  provides otherwise, applies in respect of matters pending before the
  Regulator at that time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request of Regulator

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may, in order to request the issuance
  of a direction under subsection (2), suspend the determination of any
  matter of which it is seized.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2019, c. 28, s. 10 "374"
- [2020, c. 1, s. 209](#1238421-1253170){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1238421-1253170.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-374-20190828.html "Link to previous version of section 374"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration of Governor in Council

**[[375]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-375 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, by order, declare that the maintenance or introduction of a
restriction on the exportation to Chile or Costa Rica of energy goods or
of any quality, kind or class of energy goods is justified under Article
C-13 of the CCFTA or Article III.11 of the CCRFTA, as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2019, c. 28, s. 10 "375"
- [2020, c. 1, s. 210](#1238423-1253175){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1238423-1253175.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-375-20190828.html "Link to previous version of section 375"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for declaration

**[[376]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-376 .sectionLabel}** If, in the course of
determining an application for a licence or permit, or determining
whether to make an order, for the exportation to Chile or Costa Rica of
energy goods or of any quality, kind or class of energy goods, the
Commission considers that the maintenance or introduction of a
restriction on that exportation is in the public interest and that
subparagraph 1(a), (b) or (c) of Article C-13 of the CCFTA or
subparagraph 1(a), (b) or (c) of Article III.11 of the CCRFTA, as the
case may be, would apply as a consequence of the restriction, the
Commission may, in order to request that the Minister recommend to the
Governor in Council that an order be made under section 375 in respect
of the relevant energy goods, suspend the determination until not later
than 120 days after the day on which the request is made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2019, c. 28, s. 10 "376"
- [2020, c. 1, s. 210](#1238423-1253180){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1238423-1253180.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-376-20190828.html "Link to previous version of section 376"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exportation to Chile or Costa Rica

- **[[377]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-377 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commission may neither refuse to issue a licence or permit or make an
  order nor suspend, revoke or vary a licence, permit or order for the
  exportation to Chile or Costa Rica of energy goods or of any quality,
  kind or class of energy goods if that refusal, revocation, suspension
  or variation would constitute the maintenance or introduction of a
  restriction on that exportation as a consequence of which subparagraph
  1(a), (b) or (c) of Article C-13 of the CCFTA or subparagraph
  1(a), (b) or (c) of Article III.11 of the CCRFTA, as the case may be,
  would apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of the
  exportation to Chile or Costa Rica of any energy goods, or of any
  quality, kind or class of energy goods, referred to in an order made
  under section 375 while the order is in force.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application or consent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the Commission may suspend,
  revoke or vary a licence, permit or order if the holder of the
  licence, the holder of the permit or the person named in the order, as
  the case may be, applies for or consents to the suspension or
  revocation.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2019, c. 28, s. 10 "377"
- [2020, c. 1, s. 211](#1238425-1253187){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1238425-1253187.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-377-20190828.html "Link to previous version of section 377"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No declaration made

**[[378]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-378 .sectionLabel}** Despite not being
satisfied that the criteria set out in section 345 have been met, the
Commission may issue a licence for the exportation to Chile or Costa
Rica of any energy goods, or of any quality, kind or class of energy
goods, referred to in a request made under section 376 if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister declines to recommend to the Governor in
  Council that an order be made under section 375;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Governor in Council declines to make the order;
  or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} no order is made within 120 days after the day on
  which the request is made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2019, c. 28, s. 10 "378"
- [2020, c. 1, s. 212](#1238427-1253191){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1238427-1253191.html"}
:::

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Offences and Punishment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1164363 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

- **[[379]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-379
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes any of
  the provisions of this Part or the regulations made under this Part is
  guilty of an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding
    \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one year, or
    to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine not
    exceeding \$1,000,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    five years, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Due diligence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person is not to be found guilty of an offence
  under subsection (1) if they establish that they exercised due
  diligence to prevent the commission of the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officers, directors, agents and
  mandataries

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a corporation commits an offence under subsection
  (1), any officer or director, or agent or mandatary, of the
  corporation who directed, authorized, assented to, acquiesced in or
  participated in the commission of the offence is a party to and guilty
  of the offence and is liable on conviction to the punishment provided
  for the offence whether or not the corporation has been prosecuted or
  convicted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of offence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In any prosecution for an offence under subsection
  (1), it is sufficient proof of the offence to show that it was
  committed by an employee, or an agent or mandatary, of the accused,
  whether or not the employee, or agent or mandatary, is identified or
  has been prosecuted for the offence, unless the accused establishes
  that the offence was committed without the knowledge or consent of the
  accused and that the accused exercised due diligence to prevent its
  commission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing offence

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If an offence under subsection (1) is committed on
  more than one day, it is deemed to be a separate offence for each day
  on which the offence is committed or continued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period or prescription

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Proceedings by way of summary conviction in respect
  of an offence under subsection (1) may be instituted at any time
  within but not later than one year after the day on which the subject
  matter of the proceedings arose.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of certain officers

**[[380]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-380 .sectionLabel}** An
[[officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1) of the
[Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) has, in respect of the exportation or
importation of oil or gas, all the powers of an officer under the
[Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) and, except to the extent that they are
inconsistent with any regulations made under paragraph 353(1)(e), the
provisions of that Act and any regulations made under that Act
respecting search, detention, seizure, forfeiture, condemnation and
disposition apply, with any modifications that the circumstances
require, to oil or gas tendered for exportation or importation or
exported or imported or otherwise dealt with contrary to this Part.

## [PART 8]{.HLabel1}[Oil and Gas Interests, Production and Conservation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1164381 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1164382 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions of
[[oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} and [[gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[381]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-381 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Part, [[oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} and [[gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
have the same meaning as in subsection 2(1) of the [Canada Oil and Gas
Operations Act](/eng/acts/O-7).

### [Declarations of Significant Discovery and Commercial Discovery]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1164385 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[382]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-382 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  section applies to any decision of the Commission to make, amend or
  revoke a declaration of significant discovery under section 28 of the
  [Canada Petroleum Resources Act](/eng/acts/C-8.5) or a declaration of
  commercial discovery under section 35 of that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At least 30 days before making a decision to which
  this section applies, the Commission must give written notice of its
  intention to make the decision to any person the Commission considers
  to be directly affected by the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for hearing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person to whom notice is given may, in writing,
  request a hearing in respect of the decision, but the request must be
  received by the Commission within 30 days after the day on which
  notice is given.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No request for hearing

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If no request is received in accordance with
  subsection (3), the Commission may make the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a request is received in accordance with
  subsection (3), the Commission must fix a time and place for the
  hearing and notify each person who requested the hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Each person who requests a hearing may make
  representations and introduce witnesses and documents at the hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Commission must make its decision and give notice
  of that decision to each person who requested the hearing and, on that
  person's request, the Commission must make the reasons for its
  decision public.

### [Operating Licences and Authorizations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1164401 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation of licences or authorizations

**[[383]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-383 .sectionLabel}** The Commission or a
person to whom powers are delegated under section 4.1 of the [Canada Oil
and Gas Operations Act](/eng/acts/O-7) may vary the conditions of any
operating licence or authorization issued under section 5 of that Act.

### [Chief Conservation Officer and Chief Safety Officer]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1164404 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to appeals

- **[[384]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-384 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  section applies to appeals brought under section 21 or subsection
  25(8) of the [Canada Oil and Gas Operations Act](/eng/acts/O-7) by a
  person aggrieved by an order of the Chief Conservation Officer or by
  any action or measure taken or authorized or directed to be taken by
  that Officer, other than the referral of an order to the Commission
  under subsection 58(5) of that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After hearing an appeal to which this section
  applies, the Commission may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} set aside, confirm or vary the order, action or
    measure that is the subject of the appeal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order any works to be undertaken that the
    Commission considers necessary to prevent waste, the escape of oil
    or gas or any other contravention of the [Canada Oil and Gas
    Operations Act](/eng/acts/O-7) or the regulations made under that
    Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} make any other order that the Commission considers
    appropriate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Show cause hearing relating to waste

- **[[385]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-385 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  section applies to applications by the Chief Conservation Officer to
  the Commission under section 22 of the [Canada Oil and Gas Operations
  Act](/eng/acts/O-7) for a show cause hearing relating to
  [[waste]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in paragraph 18(2)(f)
  or (g) of that Act, in the recovery of oil or gas from a pool.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On receiving an application, the Commission must make
  an order requiring the operators within the pool to show cause at a
  hearing, to be held on a day specified in the order, why the
  Commission should not make a direction in respect of the waste.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On the day specified in the order, the Commission
  must hold a hearing at which the Chief Conservation Officer, the
  operators and other interested persons must be given an opportunity to
  be heard.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, after the hearing, the Commission considers that
  waste is occurring in the recovery of oil or gas from a pool, it may,
  by order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} direct the introduction of a scheme for the
    collection, processing, disposition or reinjection of any gas
    produced from the pool; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} direct repressurizing, recycling or pressure
    maintenance for the pool or any part of the pool and for or
    incidental to that purpose, direct the introduction or injection of
    gas, water or any other substance into the pool or any part of the
    pool.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional order

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In addition to making an order under subsection (4),
  the Commission may, by order, direct that the pool or any part of the
  pool specified in the order be shut in if the requirements of the
  order are not met or unless a scheme is approved by the Commission and
  in operation by a date fixed in the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation pending approval

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (4) and (5), the Commission may,
  by order, permit the continued operation of a pool or any part of a
  pool after the date fixed by an order made under this section if the
  Commission considers that a scheme or other action described in
  paragraph (4)(a) or (b) is in the course of preparation, but the
  continued operation is subject to any conditions the Regulator
  imposes.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[386]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-386 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  section applies to an order referred by the Chief Safety Officer or
  the Chief Conservation Officer to the Commission under subsection
  58(5) of the [Canada Oil and Gas Operations Act](/eng/acts/O-7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review and decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commission must review the need for the order and
  may confirm it or set it aside.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The burden of establishing that the order is not
  needed is on the person who requested that the order be referred to
  the Commission.

### [Orders]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1164435 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

- **[[387]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-387
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who fails to comply
  with an order of the Commission under section 384 or 385 is guilty of
  an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding
    \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one year, or
    to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine not
    exceeding \$1,000,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    five years, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Due diligence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person is not to be found guilty of an offence
  under subsection (1) if they establish that they exercised due
  diligence to prevent the commission of the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicable provisions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Sections 65 to 71 of the [Canada Oil and Gas
  Operations Act](/eng/acts/O-7) apply, with any modifications that the
  circumstances require, in respect of an offence under subsection (1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

**[[388]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-388 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
an order of the Commission made under this Part is not a [[statutory
instrument]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1) of the
[Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

## [PART 9]{.HLabel1}[General]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1164447 .Part}

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1164448 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations respecting accounts, etc.

- **[[389]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-389 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator may, with the approval of the Governor in Council, make
  regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting the manner in which the accounts of a
    company must be kept;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the classes of property for which
    depreciation charges may properly be included under operating
    expenses in the accounts and the accounting methods that may be used
    in computing and charging depreciation in respect of each of the
    classes of property;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting a uniform system of accounts applicable
    to any class of company; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} requiring that any of the following persons and
    companies keep and make available for inspection, in the form that
    may be prescribed by the regulations, at a place of business in
    Canada any records, books of account and other documents and submit
    to the Regulator, at any time and in any form that may be
    prescribed, returns and information respecting capital, traffic,
    revenues, expenses and other matters that are prescribed and that
    the Regulator determines are matters that should be considered by it
    in carrying out its powers and duties under this Act in relation to
    those companies and persons

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} companies that have been authorized to construct
      or operate a pipeline,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} companies that have been authorized to abandon
      the operation of a pipeline under subsection 241(1),

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} persons exporting oil, gas or electricity, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} persons holding a licence under Part 7.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may, by order, on any conditions it
  considers appropriate, exempt a company or person from the application
  of a regulation made under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes a regulation made under
  this section is guilty of an offence punishable on summary conviction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Due diligence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person is not to be found guilty of an offence
  under subsection (3) if they establish that they exercised due
  diligence to prevent the commission of the offence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[390]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-390 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may make regulations for the purposes of this Act,
  including regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designating as an oil product or as a gas product
    any substance resulting from the processing or refining of
    hydrocarbons or coal if that substance

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is asphalt or a lubricant, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is a source of energy by itself or when it is
      combined or used in association with something else; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exempting any oil or gas or any kind, quality or
    class of oil or gas or any area or transaction from all or any of
    the provisions of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Regulations may only be made under paragraph (1)(b)
  after consultation with the Regulator.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security regulations

- **[[391]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-391 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator may, with the approval of the Governor in Council, make
  regulations respecting the security of pipelines, international power
  lines and offshore renewable energy projects, including regulations
  respecting standards, plans and audits.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes a regulation made under
  subsection (1) is guilty of an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding
    \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one year or to
    both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine not
    exceeding \$500,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding five
    years or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Due diligence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person is not to be found guilty of an offence
  under subsection (2) if they establish that they exercised due
  diligence to prevent the commission of the offence.

### [Review of Act]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1164484 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of Act after 10 years

**[[392]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-392 .sectionLabel}** Ten years after the day
on which this Act comes into force, a comprehensive review of the
provisions and operation of the Act is to be undertaken by the committee
of the Senate, of the House of Commons or of both Houses of Parliament
that is designated or established by the Senate or the House of Commons,
or by both Houses of Parliament, as the case may be, for that purpose.

## RELATED PROVISIONS {#related-provisions .scheduleTitleText}

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 11

  - ###### Definitions {#definitions .MarginalNote}

    - [11]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply
      in this section and sections 12 to 43.

      [[commencement day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[commencement day]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the day on
          which the [Canadian Energy Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1)
          comes into force. ([date d'entrée en vigueur]{.DefinedTermLink
          lang="fr"})

      [[National Energy Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[National Energy Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
          National Energy Board established by section 3 of the
          [National Energy Board Act](/eng/acts/N-7) as it read
          immediately before the commencement
          day. ([Office]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

      [[Regulator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

      :   [[Regulator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canadian Energy
          Regulator established by section 10 of the [Canadian Energy
          Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1). ([Régie]{.DefinedTermLink
          lang="fr"})

    - ###### Terminology --- [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) {#terminology-federal-public-sector-labour-relations-act .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless the context requires otherwise, words and
      expressions used in sections 16 to 32 have the same meaning as in
      section 2 of the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3).

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 12

  - ###### Appointments terminated {#appointments-terminated .MarginalNote}

    - [12]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each permanent member of the
      National Energy Board appointed under subsection 3(2) of the
      [National Energy Board Act](/eng/acts/N-7), as it read immediately
      before the commencement day, who holds office immediately before
      the commencement day ceases to hold office on that day.

    - ###### Compensation {#compensation .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} A permanent member of the National Energy Board
      referred to in subsection (1) who is not appointed as a
      commissioner under subsection 28(1) of the [Canadian Energy
      Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1) is entitled to the lesser of

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an amount that is equal to 12 months'
        remuneration as a permanent member of the National Energy Board,
        and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an amount that is equal to the remuneration to
        which they would be entitled if they were to complete the
        remainder of the term for which they were appointed as a
        permanent member of the National Energy Board.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 13

  - ###### Appointments terminated {#appointments-terminated-1 .MarginalNote}

    - [13]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each temporary member of the
      National Energy Board appointed under subsection 4(1) of the
      [National Energy Board Act](/eng/acts/N-7), as it read immediately
      before the commencement day, who holds office immediately before
      the commencement day ceases to hold office on that day.

    - ###### No compensation {#no-compensation .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite the provisions of any contract, agreement
      or order, no person who was appointed to hold office as a
      temporary member of the National Energy Board has any right to
      claim or receive any compensation, damages, indemnity or other
      form of relief from Her Majesty in right of Canada or from any
      employee or agent of Her Majesty for ceasing to hold that office
      or for the abolition of that office by the operation of this Act.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 14

  - ###### Continuation --- members of National Energy Board {#continuation-members-of-national-energy-board .MarginalNote}

    - [14]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person referred to in
      subsection 12(1) or 13(1) may, at the request of the Lead
      Commissioner designated under 37 of the [Canadian Energy Regulator
      Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1), continue to hear and decide any matter
      that was before the person before the commencement day and the
      person is considered to be a commissioner for the purposes of that
      Act.

    - ###### Refusal to complete duties {#refusal-to-complete-duties .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person referred to in subsection 12(1) or
      13(1) refuses to continue to hear or decide a matter referred to
      in subsection (1), the Commission referred to in section 26 of the
      [Canadian Energy Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1) is seized of the
      matter.

    - ###### Remuneration and expenses {#remuneration-and-expenses .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person referred to in subsection 12(1) or 13(1)
      who continues to hear and decide a matter referred to in
      subsection (1)

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is to be paid the remuneration for their
        services that may be fixed by the Governor in Council; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is entitled to be paid reasonable travel and
        other expenses incurred in the course of providing services
        during any period of absence from their ordinary place of
        residence.

    - ###### Limitation {#limitation .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Lead Commissioner may withdraw from a person
      referred to in subsection 12(1) or 13(1) a matter referred to in
      subsection (1) that is not disposed of within one year after the
      commencement day and assign it to a panel of the Commission in
      accordance with section 38 of the [Canadian Energy Regulator
      Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1) on any terms and conditions that the Lead
      Commissioner may specify for the protection and preservation of
      the rights and interests of the parties.

    - ###### Subsection 28(2) of [Canadian Energy Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1) {#subsection-282-of-canadian-energy-regulator-act .MarginalNote}

      [(5)]{.lawlabel} A period of time during which a person referred
      to in subsection 12(1) or 13(1) is considered to be a commissioner
      for the purposes of this section does not count as part of the
      10-year limit set out in subsection 28(2) of the [Canadian Energy
      Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1).

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 15

  - ###### Persons employed by National Energy Board {#persons-employed-by-national-energy-board .MarginalNote}

    [15]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this Act affects the status of any person
    who was employed by the National Energy Board immediately before the
    commencement day, except that, as of that day, the person is
    employed by the Regulator.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 16

  - ###### Collective agreements or arbitral awards continued {#collective-agreements-or-arbitral-awards-continued .MarginalNote}

    - [16]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 17 to 28,
      every collective agreement or arbitral award that applies to an
      employee who occupies a position in the National Energy Board
      before the commencement day and that is in force immediately
      before that day continues in force until its term expires.

    - ###### Binding effect {#binding-effect .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} A collective agreement or arbitral award
      continued in force under subsection (1) is binding on

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Regulator, as if it were the employer
        referred to in the collective agreement or arbitral award;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the bargaining agent that is a party to the
        collective agreement or arbitral award; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employees of the Regulator in the
        bargaining unit in respect of which that bargaining agent has
        been certified.

    - ###### Parties may amend {#parties-may-amend .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsections (1) and (2) prohibits the
      Regulator and the bargaining agent from amending any provision of
      a collective agreement continued in force under subsection (1),
      other than a provision relating to its term.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 17

  - ###### Application for certification {#application-for-certification .MarginalNote}

    [17]{.lawlabel} Any employee organization may apply to the Board for
    certification as the bargaining agent for the employees bound by a
    collective agreement or arbitral award that is continued in force
    under subsection 16(1), but it may do so only during the period in
    which an application for certification is authorized to be made
    under section 54 of the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations
    Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) in respect of those employees.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 18

  - ###### Power of Board {#power-of-board .MarginalNote}

    - [18]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a collective agreement or
      arbitral award is continued in force under subsection 16(1), the
      Board must, by order, on application by the Regulator or any
      bargaining agent affected by the establishment of the Regulator,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine whether the employees of the
        Regulator who are bound by the collective agreement or arbitral
        award constitute one or more units appropriate for collective
        bargaining;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} determine which employee organization is to be
        the bargaining agent for the employees in each such unit; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of each collective agreement or
        arbitral award that binds employees of the Regulator, determine
        whether the collective agreement or arbitral award is to remain
        in force and, if it is to remain in force, determine whether it
        is to remain in force until the expiry of its term or until an
        earlier date that the Board may fix.

    - ###### When application must be made {#when-application-must-be-made .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application may be made only during the
      period beginning on the 120th day after the commencement day and
      ending on the 150th day after the commencement day.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 19

  - ###### Application for leave to give notice to bargain collectively {#application-for-leave-to-give-notice-to-bargain-collectively .MarginalNote}

    - [19]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Either of the parties to a
      collective agreement or arbitral award that remains in force by
      reason of an order made under paragraph 18(1)(c) may apply to the
      Board for an order granting leave to give to the other party,
      under section 105 of the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), a notice to bargain collectively.

    - ###### When application may be made {#when-application-may-be-made .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must be made within 90 days after
      the day on which the order is made under paragraph 18(1)(c).

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 20

  - ###### No application within specified period {#no-application-within-specified-period .MarginalNote}

    - [20]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} If no application for an order
      under subsection 18(1) is made within the period specified in
      subsection 18(2), the Regulator or any bargaining agent bound by a
      collective agreement or arbitral award that is continued in force
      under subsection 16(1) may apply to the Board for an order
      granting leave to give to the other party, under section 105 of
      the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), a notice to bargain collectively.

    - ###### When application may be made {#when-application-may-be-made-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application may be made only during the
      period beginning on the 151st day after the commencement day and
      ending on the 240th day after the commencement day.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 21

  - ###### Notice to bargain given before commencement day {#notice-to-bargain-given-before-commencement-day .MarginalNote}

    [21]{.lawlabel} A notice to bargain collectively that was given
    before the commencement day does not bind the Regulator and a new
    notice to bargain collectively may be given only in the
    circumstances set out in paragraph 23(b).

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 22

  - ###### Duty to observe conditions {#duty-to-observe-conditions .MarginalNote}

    [22]{.lawlabel} If a notice to bargain collectively is given before
    the commencement day, then, unless the Regulator and the bargaining
    agent agree otherwise, the conditions of employment continued in
    force under section 107 of the [Federal Public Sector Labour
    Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) are binding on the Regulator, the
    bargaining agent for the bargaining unit and the employees in the
    bargaining unit from the commencement day until

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if no application is made under paragraph 23(a),
      the expiry of 150 days after the commencement day; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if such an application is made, the day the
      notice referred to in paragraph 23(b) is given.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 23

  - ###### Application and notice to bargain collectively {#application-and-notice-to-bargain-collectively .MarginalNote}

    [23]{.lawlabel} If a notice to bargain collectively is given before
    the commencement day,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on application by the Regulator or by the
      bargaining agent, made during the period beginning on the 120th
      day after the commencement day and ending on the 150th day after
      that day, the Board must make an order determining

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} whether the employees of the Regulator who are
        represented by the bargaining agent constitute one or more units
        appropriate for collective bargaining, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} which employee organization is to be the
        bargaining agent for the employees in each such unit; and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the Board makes the determinations under
      paragraph (a), the Regulator or the bargaining agent may, by
      notice given under section 105 of the [Federal Public Sector
      Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), require the other to
      commence collective bargaining for the purpose of entering into a
      collective agreement.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 24

  - ###### Inquiry and votes {#inquiry-and-votes .MarginalNote}

    [24]{.lawlabel} Before making an order under subsection 18(1) or
    paragraph 23(a), the Board may make any inquiry or direct that a
    representation vote be taken among the employees to be affected by
    the order.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 25

  - ###### Consideration of classification {#consideration-of-classification .MarginalNote}

    - [25]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs
      18(1)(a) and 23(a), in determining whether a group of employees
      constitutes a unit appropriate for collective bargaining, the
      Board must have regard to the Regulator's classification of
      persons and positions, including the occupational groups or
      subgroups established by it.

    - ###### Unit co-extensive with occupational groups {#unit-co-extensive-with-occupational-groups .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board must establish bargaining units that
      are co-extensive with the occupational groups or subgroups
      established by the Regulator, unless doing so would not permit
      satisfactory representation of the employees to be included in a
      particular bargaining unit and, for that reason, such a unit would
      not be appropriate for collective bargaining.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 26

  - ###### Determination of questions of membership in bargaining units {#determination-of-questions-of-membership-in-bargaining-units .MarginalNote}

    [26]{.lawlabel} On application by the Regulator or the employee
    organization affected by the establishment of the Regulator, the
    Regulator must determine every question that arises as to whether
    any employee or class of employees is included in a bargaining unit
    determined by the Board under paragraph 18(1)(a) or 23(a) to
    constitute a unit appropriate for collective bargaining or is
    included in any other unit.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 27

  - ###### Employer participation {#employer-participation .MarginalNote}

    - [27]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Board is not authorized to
      determine that an employee organization is to be a bargaining
      agent under paragraph 18(1)(b) or 23(a) if it considers that the
      Regulator, or a person acting on behalf of the Regulator, has
      participated or is participating in the formation or
      administration of the employee organization in a manner that
      impairs its fitness to represent the interests of the employees in
      the bargaining unit.

    - ###### Discrimination {#discrimination .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board is not authorized to determine that an
      employee organization is to be a bargaining agent under paragraph
      18(1)(b) or 23(a) if it discriminates against any employee on a
      prohibited ground of discrimination within the meaning of the
      [Canadian Human Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6).

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 28

  - ###### Application of [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) {#application-of-federal-public-sector-labour-relations-act .MarginalNote}

    - [28]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The provisions of Part 1 of the
      [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3),
      and any rules or regulations made under that Act, apply to, or in
      respect of, the following and any matter related to the following:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an application made to the Board under any of
        sections 17 to 20, 23 and 26;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order made by the Board under any of
        sections 18 to 20 and 23;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a determination of the Board made under any of
        sections 18, 23 and 26 and a bargaining unit, bargaining agent
        or employee or class of employees that is the subject of such a
        determination;

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a collective agreement or arbitral award that
        is continued in force under subsection 16(1); and

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} collective bargaining that is commenced after
        the receipt of a notice referred to in section 19 or 20 or
        paragraph 23(b) and a collective agreement that is entered into
        following such collective bargaining.

    - ###### Powers, duties and functions of Board {#powers-duties-and-functions-of-board .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Board has, for the purposes of performing its
      duties and functions under any of sections 17 to 27, the powers
      conferred on it under Part 1 of the [Federal Public Sector Labour
      Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), and it must perform the duties
      and functions imposed on it under that Part in respect of those
      powers.

    - ###### Inconsistency {#inconsistency .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the event of any inconsistency between
      sections 17 to 27 and the provisions of Part 1 of the [Federal
      Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), or anything
      issued, made or established under that Act, those sections 17 to
      27 prevail to the extent of the inconsistency.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 29

  - ###### Persons not represented {#persons-not-represented .MarginalNote}

    [29]{.lawlabel} The conditions of employment of persons who are not
    represented by a bargaining agent or who are excluded from a
    bargaining unit and who, on the commencement day, occupy their
    position within the Regulator continue to apply until new conditions
    of employment for those persons are established.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 30

  - ###### Complaints {#complaints .MarginalNote}

    [30]{.lawlabel} The provisions of Division 13 of Part 1 of the
    [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) and
    any rules or regulations made under that Act, as they read
    immediately before the commencement day, continue to apply in
    respect of any complaint made under that Division before that day
    that relates to the National Energy Board.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 31

  - ###### Grievances {#grievances .MarginalNote}

    - [31]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The provisions of Part 2 of the
      [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) and
      any rules or regulations made under that Act, as they read
      immediately before the commencement day, continue to apply in
      respect of any grievance presented under that Part before that day
      by an employee of the National Energy Board.

    - ###### Implementation of decision {#implementation-of-decision .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} A final decision with respect to a grievance
      referred to in subsection (1) that provides for the reinstatement
      of an employee or the payment of money to an employee must be
      implemented by the Regulator as soon as feasible.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 32

  - ###### Matter referred to Board {#matter-referred-to-board .MarginalNote}

    [32]{.lawlabel} The provisions Part 2 of the [Federal Public Sector
    Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3) and any rules or regulations
    made under that Act, as they read immediately before the
    commencement day, continue to apply in respect of any matter
    referred to the Board under that Part before that day that relates
    to the National Energy Board.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 33

  - ###### Decisions and orders {#decisions-and-orders .MarginalNote}

    [33]{.lawlabel} Every decision or order made by the National Energy
    Board is considered to have been made under the [Canadian Energy
    Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1) and may be enforced as such.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 34

  - ###### Certificates, licences and permits {#certificates-licences-and-permits .MarginalNote}

    - [34]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every certificate, licence or
      permit issued by the National Energy Board is considered to have
      been issued under the [Canadian Energy Regulator
      Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1) and, unless suspended or revoked under that
      Act, every certificate, licence or permit remains in force for the
      remainder of the period during which it would have been in force
      had the [Canadian Energy Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1) not come
      into force.

    - ###### Suspensions {#suspensions .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every certificate, licence or permit referred to
      in subsection (1) that was suspended immediately before the
      commencement day is considered to be suspended under the [Canadian
      Energy Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1).

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 35

  - ###### Leave {#leave .MarginalNote}

    [35]{.lawlabel} Any leave granted by the National Energy Board is
    considered to have been granted under the [Canadian Energy Regulator
    Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1).

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 36

  - ###### Pending applications {#pending-applications .MarginalNote}

    [36]{.lawlabel} Applications pending before the National Energy
    Board immediately before the commencement day are to be taken up
    before the Commission of the Regulator and continued in accordance
    with the [National Energy Board Act](/eng/acts/N-7) as it read
    immediately before the commencement day.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 36.1

  - ###### For greater certainty {#for-greater-certainty .MarginalNote}

    [36.1]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, section 182.1 of the
    [Impact Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75) applies in relation to a
    pending application referred to in section 36.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 37

  - ###### Excluded periods if no regulations --- Part 3 {#excluded-periods-if-no-regulations-part-3 .MarginalNote}

    - [37]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before the coming into force of
      the first regulation made for the purposes of subsection 183(5) or
      214(5) of the [Canadian Energy Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1),
      the Lead Commissioner may, in the circumstances that he or she
      considers appropriate, exercise the powers set out in those
      subsections.

    - ###### Excluded periods if no regulations --- Part 4 {#excluded-periods-if-no-regulations-part-4 .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before the coming into force of the first
      regulation made for the purposes of subsection 262(6) of the
      [Canadian Energy Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1), the Lead
      Commissioner may, in the circumstances that he or she considers
      appropriate, exercise the power set out in that subsection.

    - ###### Excluded periods if no regulations --- Part 5 {#excluded-periods-if-no-regulations-part-5 .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before the coming into force of the first
      regulation made for the purposes of subsection 298(6) of the
      [Canadian Energy Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1), the Lead
      Commissioner may, in the circumstances that he or she considers
      appropriate, exercise the power set out in that subsection.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 38

  - ###### Certified copies {#certified-copies .MarginalNote}

    [38]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of sections 198, 208 and 265 of the
    [Canadian Energy Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1), a copy of a
    document that is certified by the Secretary of the National Energy
    Board is considered to have been certified by the Regulator under
    that Act.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 39

  - ###### Lands --- consent not required {#lands-consent-not-required .MarginalNote}

    [39]{.lawlabel} A person or company is not required to obtain the
    consent referred to in subsection 317(1) of the [Canadian Energy
    Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1) in relation to lands if, before the
    commencement day,

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person or company had taken possession of,
      used or occupied those lands; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person or company had obtained the
      authorization under the [National Energy Board
      Act](/eng/acts/N-7), as it read immediately before the
      commencement day, to construct or operate a pipeline or power line
      on those lands.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 40

  - ###### Section 112 of [National Energy Board Act](/eng/acts/N-7) {#section-112-of-national-energy-board-act .MarginalNote}

    [40]{.lawlabel} Section 112 of the [National Energy Board
    Act](/eng/acts/N-7), as it read immediately before the commencement
    day, continues to apply in relation to compensation matters in
    respect of a pipeline for which

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an application for a certificate under subsection
      52(1) of that Act was received by the National Energy Board before
      that day; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an application for an order under subsection
      58(1) of that Act was received by the National Energy Board before
      that day.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 41

  - ###### Pending requests {#pending-requests .MarginalNote}

    [41]{.lawlabel} Any request for which a notice was served on the
    Minister under subsections 88(1) and 90(1) of the [National Energy
    Board Act](/eng/acts/N-7), as those sections read immediately before
    the commencement day, is continued in accordance with those
    subsections.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 42

  - ###### Importation of oil or gas {#importation-of-oil-or-gas .MarginalNote}

    - [42]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Division I of Part VI of the
      [National Energy Board Act](/eng/acts/N-7), as it read immediately
      before the commencement day, continues to apply to the importation
      of oil or gas until the earlier of the third anniversary of the
      commencement day and the day on which regulations are made for the
      purposes of section 352 of the [Canadian Energy Regulator
      Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1).

    - ###### Interpretation {#interpretation .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), every
      reference to the National Energy Board in Division I of Part VI of
      the [National Energy Board Act](/eng/acts/N-7) is to be read as a
      reference to the Regulator or the Commission of the Regulator, as
      the case may be.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 42.1

  - ###### References {#references .MarginalNote}

    [42.1]{.lawlabel} Every reference to the National Energy Board in
    any deed, contract, agreement or other document executed, or in
    Quebec, signed, by the National Energy Board in its own name is,
    unless the context otherwise requires, to be read as a reference to
    the Regulator.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 42.2

  - ###### Appropriations {#appropriations .MarginalNote}

    [42.2]{.lawlabel} Any amount that is appropriated by an Act of
    Parliament for the fiscal year in which the commencement day falls
    to defray the expenditures of the National Energy Board and that is
    unexpended on that day is considered to be an amount appropriated to
    defray the expenditures of the Regulator.

- --- 2019, c. 28, s. 43

  - ###### Regulations {#regulations .MarginalNote}

    [43]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make any regulations
    that the Governor in Council considers necessary to provide for any
    other transitional matter arising from the coming into force of the
    [Canadian Energy Regulator Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1).
:::
